# Research log

Detailed notes on completed research passes, reviews and source investigations. Entries are kept
as originally written, so counts and statuses are as of each entry; the [README](../README.md) and
[roadmap](roadmap.md) give the current state. Each campaign's first pass also has its own record in
[`docs/research/`](research/) (`*-first-pass-v1.md`), and review responses are under
[`artifacts/review-results/`](../artifacts/review-results/).

Terms used below: *the primary* is the main AI agent that prepares and maintains the evidence, as
distinct from the separate reviewer; *the owner* is the project maintainer; *Astra `xhigh`* and
*Opus 5.5 `high`* are the separate AI reviewers (GPT-6 Astra at `xhigh` reasoning effort until
2026-09-24, then Claude Opus 5.5 at `high`). *Frozen* records and campaign groups are those of the
127-engagement 1862–1863 pilot cohort.

## Hundred Days 1815 complete first pass, 2026-10-19

The [campaign record](research/hundred-days-1815-first-pass-v1.md) adds **15/15 drafts, 228 claims, 65 explicit unknowns and 254 citations**, covering nine dimensions and 30 side records. This completes **325/325 first-cohort drafts across 16/16 complete groups**, with 4,846 claims and 1,085 explicit unknowns. Italy, Belgium and the frontier sieges retain their full frozen intervals. Troop/population alternatives, delayed arrivals, local/superior command, Huningue’s internal mismatch and separate capitulation/departure milestones remain explicit.

Pre-reading family assignments match every final three-family set. Five entries used one bounded targeted check each; the others none. Primary verification corrected Plotho’s Chavannes estimate to his June 28 section before Belfort’s frozen opening. No fourth family, subagents, separate review or second opinion. The registry appends **226 records**, reaching **5,667**; all old 5,441 records/bytes,310 dossiers and old screen rows remain unchanged. Every adopted passage/figure was inspected on a retained image;67 narrative files bind126 exact parent contexts, and 44 manual files retain image readings.

Primary verification binds hashes, arithmetic, **29 visible digit-flag dispositions**, source/evidence checks, refreshed packet, `make reproduce`, `make check` and report inspection. Comparable coverage is complete; historical disputes, original returns, authority clocks and army identities remain unresolved. No feature admission, new historical rating/outcome fit or synthetic job; calibration remains **47 prepared/unscored**, final numerical reservation unchanged. Usage unavailable. Next recommended work: bounded strength/command ledger design using the completed evidence, before any admission or historical fit.

## Peninsula 1814 complete first pass, 2026-10-19

The [campaign record](research/peninsula-1814-first-pass-v1.md) adds **3/3 drafts, 57 claims, 4 explicit unknowns and 79 citations**, addressing nine dimensions and six side records. Orthez and Toulouse retain conflicting strength/date/population alternatives and Oman's Orthez arithmetic mismatch. Bayonne retains the whole February 23–April 18 frozen siege, separate April 14 sortie forces, Hope/Colville succession and source disagreements about the sortie result and notification date.

Pre-reading assignments stay within Bodart, Oman and Lapène. One bounded Bayonne closure cross-check; no fourth family, subagent, separate review or second opinion. The registry appends **64 records**, reaching **5,441**: 29 historical images, 20 exact-range narrative files (43 sections) and 15 manual transcriptions. Five old title/preface images were re-inspected. All old 5,377 sources and 307 dossiers remain unchanged.

Primary verification binds final hashes, assignments, arithmetic, **10 visible digit-flag dispositions**, source/evidence checks, refreshed packet, `make reproduce`, `make check` and report inspection. Coverage is **310/325 in 15/16 complete groups**, leaving **15 entries** in `hundred-days-1815 1815`. No feature admission, new rating/outcome fit, synthetic job or calibration score; calibration stays **47 prepared, unscored**, final numerical reservation unchanged. Usage unavailable. The final group was not begun here.

## Peninsula 1811 Albuera table correction, 2026-10-19

The [v2 correction](research/peninsula-1811-correction-v2.md) preserves Oman's inconsistent Albuera totals: printed summary 20,358 versus 20,650 from British/Portuguese totals. The difference 292 matches the KGL artillery row; an omitted summary row is an inference, not a repaired actual muster. The unchanged image was re-inspected, v1 source/record retained, original dossier preserved in history, and a new manual source version registered.

Current campaign totals: **19 drafts, 255 claims, 63 unknowns, 199 citations**, **162 added sources**, **4,238 registered sources overall**. The additional arithmetic claim brings the campaign to 23 digit flags, all primary-disposed. Evidence checks, `make reproduce`, `make check` and the prepared packet were refreshed. Coverage remains **170/325 in 9/16 complete groups**; no frozen/model/calibration input changed. No separate review, subagent or new source family. Next: `peninsula 1812`, eight entries. Usage unavailable.

## Fourth Coalition 1807 complete first pass, 2026-10-19

The [complete campaign record](research/fourth-coalition-1807-first-pass-v1.md) adds all
19 frozen entries: **261 claims, 53 explicit unknowns, 285 citations**. Every entry has
brief v3's nine dimensions and per-side completion checklist. Fifteen use three families,
four use two; no targeted follow-up, subagent or separate review ran. All are primary-verified.
The record gives each entry's counts, family denominator and deferred decision-relevant gap.

The pass distinguishes available, engaged, staged, duty and ration populations, superior/local
and temporary authority, and conditional agreement from actual possession. French and Prussian
result disputes and the Buenos Aires date/fortification alternatives remain visible. Höpfner's
Russian/French accounts, imperial bulletins, Groussac's trial dependence and Key-Åberg's mediated
military narrative are labelled. Actual Spanish/Swedish returns and army identities remain gaps.

There are 197 new source records: five OCR parents, ten metadata/maps, 91 inspected images
and 91 manual excerpt transcriptions. Source/section checks, research screen, `make check`
and `make reproduce` verify the batch; the packet and reports are regenerated and inspected.
The earlier 49 drafts, original source records, frozen frame/cohort, calibration and model
results retain their bytes. Coverage is **68/325 in 4/16 complete groups**, with 257 drafts
remaining; next is `peninsula 1808` (17 entries). Usage is unavailable because no trustworthy
complete token meter is exposed; it is not reported as zero or inferred from old rates.

## Shiloh source investigations

The first [Shiloh research pass](research/shiloh.md), [Confederate return
audit](research/shiloh-confederate-returns.md) and [Union availability
audit](research/shiloh-union-availability.md), and [Ohio reinforcement
audit](research/shiloh-ohio-reinforcements.md) are complete as drafts: 62 claims preserve
competing returns, reinforcement phases, dated orders and disputed responsibility. Nelson/Ammen and
regimental reports now separate crossing, landing, formation and participation. Conflicting clocks,
the untraced Sunday 600-person figure, mixed-date estimates and Reed's 7,553/7,552 discrepancy
remain explicit alongside earlier source disputes. No canonical opening strength or adjudicated
command attribution has been established. A fresh-context GPT-6 Astra `xhigh` [source
review](../artifacts/review-results/TN003-a42f063-astra-xhigh-v1/review.md) checked all 62 claims, 40
quantities, 26 events and 26 supplied scan selections. The [primary
assessment](../artifacts/review-results/TN003-a42f063-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md) accepts
four findings for a versioned correction pass: estimation provenance, section-specific document
dates, same-return dependence and Crittenden arrival wording. The [versioned correction
pass](research/shiloh-review-corrections.md) implements them with dossier schema v3 and three
source-metadata revisions. The focused review and validator follow-up are accepted. Thirty-five of
65 cited source/section pairs remain text/CSV-only. The [prepared
packet](../artifacts/research/TN003.md) and [dossier](../data/evidence/TN003.json) preserve the open
questions. Expand by complete campaign, retaining ordinary engagements and failures in the frame.

The [self-contained review handoff](research/shiloh-review-handoff.md) preserves the frozen
assignment separately from the actual response and execution record. The default reviewer is a
fresh-context Claude Opus 5.5 `high` subagent in the same task, under [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md); it
replaced GPT-6 Astra `xhigh` on 2026-09-24, and earlier Astra reviews keep their recorded scope. No
manual chat handoff is required. This AI review fulfills the bounded separate-review step, not
historical adjudication or feature admission. The [feature-admission
design](feature-admission.md) now has a separate Astra `xhigh` design review with no required
corrections. Its [13 Shiloh examples](research/shiloh-admission-examples.md) preserve source
versions, population, time and estimand restrictions. They emit no features. The [review and primary
assessment](../artifacts/review-results/feature-admission-838189f-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md)
record the exact scope and remaining historical limits.

The [offline admission validator](admission-validator.md) is implemented, with Astra `xhigh`
implementation review and focused follow-up accepted. The [review
record](../artifacts/review-results/admission-implementation-1264e21-followup-v1/primary-assessment.md)
preserves three findings and their verified fixes. Its complete-frame ledger checks all 40 Shiloh
troop observations: 18 blocked, 22 excluded and zero complete rows. No model inputs are promoted.
Actual feature release still requires reviewed boundary/population mappings and a separate immutable
admission manifest.

A separate [opening-boundary and population proposal](research/shiloh-opening-boundary-v1.md)
now uses 51 pinned passage anchors to revisit the 18 blocked observations. Its explicit v2 ledger
has **7 blocked / 33 excluded**, with no complete rows; the default v1 ledger is preserved. The
first-contact identification, mapped area and full populations remain unresolved. Astra `xhigh`
[accepted the bounded
proposal](../artifacts/review-results/shiloh-opening-330599b-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md) with
one nonblocking precision clarification; no historical feature is admitted.

The [contact-and-location packet](research/shiloh-contact-location-v1.md) adds nine participant
opening accounts, 16 inspected book-page facsimiles and two maps. It preserves conflicting contact
clocks, earlier skirmishes and map phase limits. The separate Astra `xhigh` review found two literal
transcription errors, now [corrected in new source
versions](research/shiloh-contact-location-corrections-v1.md) with primary verification. The
dossier and model inputs remain unchanged.

The [April 3–5 contact-chain audit](research/shiloh-precontact-segmentation-v1.md) recommends
April 3–4 as precursor encounters under an explicit continuity rule, with weaker event-specific
closure for April 3. The Saturday Howell link remains unresolved; no complete opening boundary or
new feature is admitted. Ten assertions bind 24 passages across 19 inspected pages. Separate Astra
`xhigh` review found one finding with two literal wording errors, now [corrected in a new source
version](research/shiloh-precontact-segmentation-corrections-v1.md) and closed by primary image
verification.

The [Howell attribution trace](research/shiloh-howell-trace-v1.md) now finds Medkirk's
retrospective Saturday account and disputed testimony selections in Worthington, and verifies Reed's
clause in the 1903 printing. Seven assertions bind 14 passages across 12 inspected pages and three
HTML sources. The source chain to Reed and Saturday-to-Sunday continuity remain unknown.

Separate Astra `xhigh` [review and primary
assessment](../artifacts/review-results/shiloh-howell-67b5c30-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md)
accepted the bounded Howell packet with no required corrections; historical provenance and
continuity questions remain open.

The [regimental and post comparison](research/shiloh-regimental-posts-v1.md) adds Reid's report
of overnight activity at an unnamed 46th Ohio picket line, Lemmon's 72d account, Worthington's post
relationships and the map cited by Medkirk. The map depicts April 6–7 phases, not Saturday's post
sequence. Seven assertions bind 21 anchors across 16 inspected pages; the registry has 151 entries /
148 raw paths at preparation. Post identity and continuity remain unresolved, with zero feature
admission.

Separate Astra `xhigh` review covered the full packet and found two literal map transcription
errors, now [corrected in a new version](research/shiloh-regimental-posts-corrections-v1.md)
and closed by primary image verification. The registry at that stage is 152 entries / 149 raw paths;
the historical conclusions and model inputs are unchanged.

The [Reid provenance trace](research/shiloh-reid-provenance-v1.md) identifies the publisher
collection process and Miller's general editorial role, but no original 46th Ohio witness. Lindsey
supplies a later wording parallel with independence unestablished. Five assertions bind 11 anchors /
10 source-locator pairs, ten retained page images and one text-only LOC catalog snapshot. All
earlier evidence is preserved; the registry at that stage has 164 entries / 161 raw paths and zero
feature admission. Separate Astra `xhigh` [review and primary
assessment](../artifacts/review-results/shiloh-reid-1c498e0-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md)
accept all five bounded assertions with two nonblocking catalog clarifications: use the retained
Agate letters label and provider page/line locators; material format and printed pagination remain
unverified.

The [Agate comparison](research/shiloh-agate-comparison-v1.md) locates two historical reprints
with 1864 title imprints, supporting a Reid/Agate account dated April 9, 1862. Its inspected opening
reports Saturday skirmishing but does not supply the later B/K overnight narrative or identify its
witness/post. The original Gazette issue remains uninspected. Five assertions bind 16 anchors / 12
source-section pairs across 11 inspected images; the registry at that packet has 176 entries / 173
raw paths, with zero feature admission. Separate Astra `xhigh` [review and primary
assessment](../artifacts/review-results/shiloh-agate-73e26d4-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md)
accept all five bounded historical assertions; the sole documentation inventory finding is corrected
and closed by primary verification. The original issue, overnight witness and continuity remain
unresolved.

## Next steps as last recorded in the README

Then define a campaign replacement boundary and outcomes before fitting enriched models. Battle
execution and campaign contribution are separate estimands; adding them together would double count.
Partial pooling, opponent/army context, measurement uncertainty, disputed-input scenarios, and a
source-exploration UI remain planned work, not implemented capabilities.

## Separate review of the early example drafts (2026-10-06)

Antietam (MD003) and Champion Hill (MS009), two of the three example drafts from the first commit,
had never had a separate review. On the owner's request, fresh-context Claude Opus 5.5 `high`
reviewers checked each against its already-inspected NPS/CWSAC records
([Antietam review](../artifacts/review-results/antietam-fb00e7d-opus-high-v1/review.md),
[Champion Hill review](../artifacts/review-results/champion-hill-fb00e7d-opus-high-v1/review.md)).

- Antietam: three required corrections. The result claim now cites the frozen "Inconclusive (Union
  strategic victory.)" text, and terrain and command-decision claims replace two unknowns. Five
  advisories were adopted, including a disputed casualty claim (23,100 in CWSAC against 22,700 on
  the NPS page). The dossier has 10 claims, 3 explicit unknowns and 18 citations (from 9, 5 and 5).
- Champion Hill: five required corrections. They cite the repeated Johnston order and the
  force-table statement, and replace all three unknowns with terrain, information and
  responsibility claims; one advisory was adopted. The corrected dossier (7 claims, no unknowns, 20
  citations, from 4) was first staged, because the frozen v1 and v2 strength and command ledgers
  bind MS009's v1 dossier by hash and the strength ledgers also bind their checker code. It was
  installed the same day under the accepted [replay design](ledger-replay.md), with all four frozen
  ledgers replaying and every authorized run reproducing byte for byte.

The primary verified every quote, with one wording change (the Champion Hill information claim) and
one added citation (Lee's CWSAC rank). Both v1 drafts are archived in `data/evidence/history/`.
Both records still rest on one source family, and no model input changes. Every dossier has now
had a separate AI review.

## Extraction audit and corrections (2026-10-06)

[Extraction audit v1](extraction-audit.md) drew a seeded random sample of 150 of the 3,116 cited
dossier claims and checked each against its passages.

- **Result:** 142 correct, 8 minor and 0 material. The material error rate's Wilson 95%
  interval is 0% to 2.5%.
- **Independence:** the auditor is the primary agent itself, not an independent sample, so errors
  the model makes systematically are not covered.
- **Corrections:** all eight minor findings were fixed in versioned dossier revisions (`LA003`,
  `MN002`, `TX001`, `VA036`, `VA058`, `VA084`, `VA096`, `VA107`), with each audited version
  archived as `.v2` in `data/evidence/history/`. All frozen ledgers still replay.
- **Review records:** every historical review record is now indexed in one disposition
  vocabulary (`artifacts/review-index.json`), and the build rejects unknown dispositions.

## Strength ledger v3 (2026-10-06)

[Strength ledger v3](ledgers-v3.md) fills grade D sides from force tables in the upstream package the
project already pins.

- **Sources:** the CWSAC Report Updates and CWSS counts, and Bodart and Clodfelter through Arnold's
  one-to-one concordances.
- **Coding:** mechanical, by table; the cell is cited, the value and class checked, and the side's
  inventory complete.
- **Copies:** an exact copy of an NPS or Livermore figure is recorded as its reproduction, so it keeps
  that figure's scope finding.
- **Exceptions:** two CWS2 cells that describe only part of a side are coded as lower bounds.
- **Result:** of 262 grade D sides, 99 are filled (A 54, B 41, C 4). Fit-eligible rows with both
  sides graded A–C rise from 126 to 181.
- **Weak points:** Bodart's whole-army totals and Clodfelter's possibly whole-army figures are left
  visible through their labels.
- **Livermore cross-check:** Arnold's machine-readable Livermore table matches the project's
  transcription on 81 of 83 sides. One difference is a v2 deliberate exclusion; the other comes from
  an untranscribed page (p.108).
- **Verification:** primary-verified; no separate review.

## Rating run 4 (2026-10-06)

[Run 4](research/commander-ratings-v4.md) answers the weaknesses that run 3's
[uncertainty analysis](../artifacts/commander-ratings-v3-uncertainty.md) found. Its
[design](commander-ratings-v4.md), code and authorization were committed in `570395c` before any
held-out result existed.

- **Changes:**
  - a theater and period context comparator;
  - τ estimated by Laplace evidence within each fold;
  - a pre-start strength view (18 post-start sides modelled, 2 keeping pre-start counts);
  - no commander term for rule-5 joint-command sides;
  - a campaign-bootstrap rule (95th percentile below zero under both weightings);
  - a war profile;
  - compact output (3.5 MB).
- **Verdict:** no improvement. Against the context model the battle-weighted difference was
  −0.0174, with its bound at −0.0054 (cleared). The campaign-weighted difference was −0.0086, with
  its bound at +0.0092 (not cleared). The point differences were both negative, which run 3's rule
  would have counted.
- **Descriptive results:**
  - the context model did worse than force size alone (+0.0111 and +0.0093);
  - the bridge to run 3's configuration on ledger v3 gives −0.0134 and −0.0073;
  - Forrest's rows carry 46% of the net gain;
  - in sample τ-hat is 0.6 (profile interval 0.2–1.0, calibrated p = 0.010);
  - Forrest's and Beauregard's 80% intervals exclude zero, and only Forrest's 95% interval does.
- **Infrastructure:**
  - grade E v2 reproduces imputation v1 exactly under run 3's settings, and the generalized fit
    reproduces `ratings.fit` exactly without context;
  - CI then showed that committed float outputs differ in the last bits on Linux and on Python 3.11,
    so those comparisons are now exact for structure and to 1e-9 for floats (`generalship/compare.py`).
- **Verification:** primary-verified; no separate review.

## Static explorer, CI and Pages (2026-10-06)

- **Explorer.** `python3 -m generalship site` writes a plain-HTML site with no dependencies. It has
  pages for every commander the command ledger names, every campaign group, every engagement and
  every registered source, plus the committed reports rendered from Markdown. An engagement page
  shows:
  - the frozen record;
  - both ledgers' entries, with their citations;
  - every dossier claim, with its quoted passage, source ID, locator, snapshot link and SHA-256.

  The pages present committed files only. `tests/test_site.py` builds the site and checks that every
  internal link and anchor resolves.
- **Hosting.** GitHub Actions runs `make check` on Python 3.11 and 3.14. A Pages workflow rebuilds
  the explorer from each pushed commit and publishes it at
  <https://masonpetrosky.github.io/generalship/>.

## Napoleonic scoping additions (2026-10-06)

The [scoping note](napoleonic-scoping.md) gains a seventh owner decision: which research dimensions
are researched in full.

- **What feeds a model.** Only strength and responsibility do: 34% of the Civil War claims and 36%
  of the citations.
- **Bodart.** The note records where Bodart's lexicon is digitized and how its Civil War totals
  compare with the ledger: 44 of 66 sides within 25%, and 7 beyond a factor of two.
- **CDB90.** Arnold's package links 49 CDB90 battles to CWSAC records, from an unpinned read.
- **Profiles.** Rating run 4's code reads a war profile, so a new war adds a profile rather than new
  model code.

## Napoleonic frame v1 (2026-10-06)

The frozen Napoleonic frame and the first research cohort are built ([design](napoleonic-frame.md)).
The work is primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Source.** Bodart's *Kriegs-Lexikon* (1908), Internet Archive `bub_gb_A0kNAAAAYAAJ`, is pinned with
  its OCR, page index, metadata and 228 page images. Pages 337–339 come from a second copy, whose scan
  shows an edge the first hides ([source additions](source-additions.md)).
- **Transcription.** Each of 227 pages was read twice, independently, by subagents (Claude Sonnet 5.5)
  under one brief, from the images alone.
  - The primary compared the readings entry by entry. It settled every disagreement and uncertain
    mark against crops of the full-resolution scan, and checked printed components against totals.
    The record is in `data/napoleonic/transcription-resolutions-v1.json`.
  - A pilot brief that allowed zooming cost about 60,000 tokens a page. The final brief, one read
    per image, cost about 8,000.
  - A seeded audit of 15 pages, 42 entries, found no frame-field error. That bounds the per-entry
    rate at 6.9% (one-sided 95%). It found one punctuation slip, corrected before the first commit.
- **Frame.** The 708 entries are classified mechanically. The rules fix:
  - the war, from the running head, checked against the belligerents;
  - membership: France's wars, 1792–1815, engagements only;
  - side A: named French troops, else a stated ally convention.

  The frame holds **663 engagements**; Bodart's winner is frozen as the outcome, and side A won 366.
  15 overrides and one running-head correction carry their reasons:
  - two misprinted years: Pojano printed 1905, Peterswalde 1809;
  - the misprinted heads of pp. 489–490;
  - five war corrections where a page head names the wrong war: Saalfeld, Amantea, Riga, Toulouse,
    Bayonne;
  - Condé;
  - three Neapolitan entries of 1815;
  - Lemnos and Copenhagen 1807, outside the frame as France was not engaged.
- **Cohort v1.** The 325 land engagements of 1805–1815 in 16 campaign groups, from
  `third-coalition 1805` (22 entries) to `hundred-days-1815 1815` (15).
- **Checks.** `make check` rebuilds the frame and cohort and fails on any difference.
  `tests/test_napoleonic.py` covers:
  - known outcomes such as Austerlitz, Waterloo and Valmy;
  - IDs and denominators;
  - the prose forms;
  - the override guards.

  Every in-frame entry's nations, side A and war, and the cohort's force and commander texts, were
  reviewed in full listings.

## Napoleonic evidence path (2026-10-06)

Recorded on 2026-10-16; the work is commit `1435766`.

- **Path.** Napoleonic drafts live in `data/napoleonic/evidence/<frame ID>.json` and are validated
  against cohort v1, with phase-record sides A and B. `python3 -m generalship
  napoleonic-evidence-check` verifies every registered source and the drafts; the build validates
  them too.
- **Brief v1.** `prompts/napoleonic-first-pass.md` applied scoping decisions 5–7:
  - each side's own public-domain sources;
  - original-language quotes with a marked English rendering;
  - figures read from registered page images;
  - strength and responsibility researched in full;
  - at most three source families per entry, Bodart included.
- **Packet.** `python3 -m generalship napoleonic-packet "<group>"` writes a campaign group's packet
  with each entry's printed Bodart text. The first was written for `third-coalition 1805`
  (22 entries).
- **Interrupted pass.** The 1805 first pass began the same evening and stopped before any dossier
  was written. It had registered 17 source records for Wertingen (B363a), uncommitted:
  - Alombert and Colin's French official history, t. III, both volumes;
  - Krauss's Austrian history of 1912;
  - selections from both;
  - six page images, each with a transcription of its figures.

## Research mode and brief v2 (2026-10-16)

- **Owner decision.** Asked what to do next, the owner set no usage cap and had the main session do
  the research itself: "Instead, let's just use no subagents. The main session should be able to
  handle everything." The [record](../data/napoleonic/owner-decision-research-mode-2026-10-16.json)
  amends scoping decision 6, which had one research agent per campaign. There is still no separate
  review; the work is primary-verified.
- **Posture and fortification.** Decision 7 named them the first candidate predictors, coded from
  the passages already read, but brief v1 and the validator never asked for them. Brief v2 adds
  both as coded dimensions, so a Napoleonic dossier covers nine.
  - `posture` codes which side took the offensive: `A_attacked`, `B_attacked` or `both_attacked`.
  - `fortification` codes which side fought from works a cited passage names: `A_fortified`,
    `B_fortified`, `both_fortified`, or `none_named` when the passages describe both positions and
    name none.
  - A known claim carries one code and an unknown claim none. The phase tags separate inherited
    works and orders from the commander's own decisions, which may be mediators.
  - `tests/test_napoleonic_evidence.py` covers the required dimensions, the code lists and codes
    on uncoded dimensions.
- **Selection check.** The source check now rebuilds every selection section from its recorded
  parent range and fails on any difference, missing or extra range, or unbound parent. All 1,586
  sections of the 431 registered selections passed when it was added. `tests/test_evidence.py`
  covers the failure cases.
- **Premise check.** Before the pass resumed, the primary counted Bodart's printed overall
  commanders in cohort v1 without reading winners. This was exploratory, with rough name matching,
  and is not committed as an artifact.
  - 82 commanders appear in two or more entries, and 30 in five or more.
  - 69 of the 82 are linked in one network of opponents.
  - 268 of the 325 entries print a figure for both sides.

## 1805 first pass, batch 1a (2026-10-17)

The first three entries of `third-coalition 1805`: Wertingen (B363a, 8 October), Günzburg (B363b,
9 October) and Haslach (B363c, 11 October). The main session researched, drafted and verified them
without subagents. The work is primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and two further families: Alombert and Colin's French official history
  (t. II and t. III vol. 1) and Krauss's Austrian history of 1912
  ([source additions](source-additions.md)). Krauss draws on Alombert and Colin for French orders
  and movements, so his French figures are not independent of theirs.
  - Every figure in a strength value or a recorded loss is read from one of 34 registered page
    images. Figures seen only in the OCR are listed as deferred in each draft's open questions.
- **Drafts.** 82 claims with 524 citations: B363a has 23, B363b 26 and B363c 33. 81 are supported
  and one is disputed; none is unknown. Every draft covers the nine dimensions.
  - Phases: 28 unresolved, 27 inherited, 12 commander-created and 15 post-outcome.
  - Posture: side A attacked at Wertingen and Günzburg. At Haslach the French accounts have the
    Austrians attack and Krauss has both sides attack, so the draft carries both codes.
  - Fortification: none is named at Wertingen or Günzburg. At Haslach the French accounts name
    Austrian entrenchments and Krauss names none.
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - Bodart's strengths against the sides' own figures. At Günzburg he gives side A 20,000,
    against the 3rd division's return of 7,069 (our sum) and Krauss's 7,000–8,000. At Haslach he
    gives side A 6,000, against 6,194 in the returns (our sum). His 25,000 for side B is the
    figure Dupont reported; Krauss estimates 18,000–20,000 infantry and 2,000–3,000 horse engaged.
  - Side A's loss at Wertingen: Bodart gives about 500 killed and wounded. Alombert and Colin give
    the cavalry reserve 19 killed and 89 wounded and Oudinot's division some thirty.
  - Haslach's result: Bodart records an Austrian victory, and Dupont's reports claim a complete
    French one; this is the batch's one disputed claim. Both histories reject or qualify Dupont.
  - Ney's report that the Austrians at Günzburg had been reinforced reads 1,500 men in Alombert
    and Colin and 15,000 in Krauss, both read from the page images.
  - Bodart's Haslach losses equal particular figures in the sources read (claim `bodart-losses`).
    But his 32% does not follow from 1,500 of 6,000, and the passages read identify 33 of his 43
    officers.
- **Gaps.** The most consequential are side B's own engaged strength and loss at Günzburg and
  Haslach, and side A's headcount nearer the day, as the returns read are of late September. The
  Austrian relations in the Kriegsarchiv files and Schönhals (1873) could close some of them.
- **Usage.** About 1.64 million tokens of the session's budget, roughly 550,000 an entry. The
  figure includes registering the three parents and learning their page structure. At that rate
  the group's 19 remaining entries would need about 10 million. Later batches reuse the
  registered sources and should keep drafts leaner, within the brief's ceiling.

## 1805 first pass, batch 1b (2026-10-17)

The Ulm phase of `third-coalition 1805`, eight entries:

| Entry | Engagement | Date (1805) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B364a | Parsdorf | 12 October | 20 |
| B364b | Elchingen | 14 October | 21 |
| B364c | Memmingen (capitulation) | 14 October | 18 |
| B364d | Herbrechtingen | 16 October | 20 |
| B365a | Ulm (capitulation) | 17 October | 19 |
| B365b | Neresheim | 17 October | 19 |
| B365d | Trochtelfingen (capitulation) | 18 October | 20 |
| B366a | Eschenau | 20 October | 19 |

The main session researched, drafted and verified them without subagents. The work is
primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and the same two families as batch 1a: Alombert and Colin, now with t. III
  vol. 2, which prints the reports and journals of the Ulm phase and Murat's pursuit; and Krauss
  ([source additions](source-additions.md)). Several of Krauss's figures for the pursuit match the
  French journals, so they are not treated as independent of them.
  - 100 records were registered, including 48 page images. Every figure in a strength value or a
    recorded loss is read from a registered page image; the drafts cite 52 figure transcriptions.
  - Figures seen only in the OCR are listed as deferred in each draft's open questions. Number
    words in the OCR now count as OCR figures too.
  - The four t. II page images of batch 1a were enlarged from 794 × 1123 pixels, not downscaled as
    their records say. The [erratum](source-additions.md) leaves the pinned records unchanged.
- **Drafts.** 156 claims with 712 citations. 153 are supported, two disputed and one unknown, and
  every draft covers the nine dimensions.
  - Disputed: Krauss dates the Parsdorf captures to 13 October, against Bodart's 12th; and
    Loison's squares of 4,000 men at Elchingen, which his own editors call exaggerated. Unknown:
    side A's loss at Trochtelfingen.
  - Phases: 61 unresolved, 37 inherited, 29 commander-created and 29 post-outcome.
  - Posture: side A attacked or pursued in all eight. The posture is commander-created at
    Parsdorf, Ulm, Neresheim and Eschenau, inherited at Elchingen and Trochtelfingen, and
    unresolved at Memmingen and Herbrechtingen.
  - Fortification: side B fought from named works at Memmingen and Ulm, and at Elchingen and
    Herbrechtingen in the French accounts, where Krauss names none. No works are named at
    Parsdorf, Neresheim, Trochtelfingen or Eschenau.
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - Bodart's strengths against the sides' own figures:

    | Engagement | Bodart | Other figures |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Elchingen, side A | 16,000 | about 8,800 engaged (Alombert and Colin) |
    | Memmingen, garrison | 6,000 | 4,500 or 4,600 (Soult's letters, Krauss) |
    | Ulm, side B | 24,000 | 22,600, or 25,000 with the sick and wounded (an Austrian return in Krauss) |
    | Trochtelfingen, side B | 1,700 | 1500 infantry and about 100 riders (Werneck, by Krauss); 6,000 infantry and 800 cavalry (Dupont's journal) |

  - Losses:
    - Ulm's prisoners: 25,365 (Alombert and Colin's regimental list), 23,000 (Austrian figures)
      or 33,000 (the bulletin).
    - Neresheim's prisoners: 1,100 (Bodart), 1000 (Murat), 2,000 (the dragoon journal) or 2000
      (Krauss).
    - Eschenau's guns: 23 (Bodart), 33 (Krauss and the dragoon record) or 35 (the cavalry
      reserve's report).
  - Command:
    - Side B's commander at Herbrechtingen: Hohenzollern in Bodart, Werneck in Krauss.
    - Who sent Liechtenstein to Ney at Ulm.
    - Whether Werneck's cavalry left before the capitulation of Trochtelfingen (Krauss,
      Hohenzollern's letter) or escaped after it, against the treaty (the French reports).
  - Times: Herbrechtingen's night attack at 9 or 11 p.m.; Neresheim's attack at 5 or 5.30 p.m.
- **Gaps.** The most consequential gap is side B's strength in five drafts: at Elchingen,
  Herbrechtingen, Neresheim and Eschenau, and what surrendered at Trochtelfingen. It is side A's
  strength at Memmingen and around Ulm, and side B's own account of Parsdorf. Austrian returns in
  Krauss's appendices or the Kriegsarchiv files could close several; none was read in this pass.
- **Usage.** About 3.0 million tokens of the session's budget, roughly 375,000 an entry. The
  figure includes registering 100 records and learning the page structure of t. III vol. 2.
  About 10.3 million remain. At this rate the group's 11 remaining entries would need about 4.1
  million, and likely more, since they need new families (Koch, Angeli, Schönhals, the
  Correspondance).

## 1805 first pass, batch 1c (2026-10-17)

The Italian engagements of `third-coalition 1805`, four entries:

| Entry | Engagement | Date (1805) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B365c | Verona | 18 October | 20 |
| B367a | Caldiero | 29–31 October | 21 |
| B367b | Pojano | 2 November | 20 |
| B369b | Castelfranco | 24 November | 19 |

The main session researched, drafted and verified them without subagents. The work is
primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and two new families ([source additions](source-additions.md)): Koch's
  *Mémoires de Masséna*, t. V (1850), the French account written from Masséna's papers, and
  Angeli's *Erzherzog Carl*, Bd. III (1897), the Austrian account written from the Kriegsarchiv
  files. Both are interested accounts. Angeli's source notes cite Koch's pages for all four
  actions, and Bodart's figures match or round Angeli's at Caldiero and Pojano, so the three are
  not fully independent here. Duhesme's report on Verona is read as Koch prints it.
  - 62 records were registered, including 29 page images. Every figure in a strength value or a
    recorded loss is read from a page image: Bodart's, through the frame transcription, or one of
    the 29. The drafts cite all 29 figure transcriptions.
  - Figures seen only in the OCR, now including ordinals, are not restated in values and are listed
    as deferred in each draft's open questions. Page numbers in Angeli's source notes are read in
    the OCR and marked so.
- **Drafts.** 80 claims with 540 citations. 79 are supported and one is disputed; none is unknown.
  Every draft covers the nine dimensions.
  - Disputed: Koch's verdict on Caldiero, an indecisive and bloody day with balanced losses,
    against the victory of side B in Bodart and Angeli.
  - Phases: 21 unresolved, 16 inherited, 27 commander-created and 16 post-outcome.
  - Posture: side A attacked at Verona and Pojano, and both sides attacked at Caldiero and
    Castelfranco; all four codes are commander-created.
  - Fortification: both sides fought from works at Verona, and side B from its entrenched position
    at Caldiero. No works are named at Pojano or Castelfranco.
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - Bodart's strengths against the sides' own figures:

    | Engagement | Bodart | Other figures |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Caldiero, side A | 46,000 | 45,000 (Angeli); 41,355 in the field divisions on 18 October (Koch's order of battle) |
    | Pojano, side B | about 4,000 | 8 battalions and 4 squadrons (Koch); 7 battalions and 2 squadrons (Angeli); 7,000 in side A's patrol reports, which Koch calls exaggerated |
    | Castelfranco, side A | 14 battalions, 12 squadrons, 9,000 | 14 battalions and 8 squadrons (Angeli) |
    | Castelfranco, side B | 4,800 | about 4000 (Angeli); 6,000 infantry and 1,000 cavalry taken (Koch) |

  - Losses:
    - Verona, side B: 1,200 with 800 prisoners (Bodart), 1,800 (Koch), 1,500 prisoners (Duhesme)
      or 1622 at S. Giorgio and 76 at Veronetta (Angeli). Side A: about 900 (Bodart), or 150
      killed and 300 wounded (Koch, and Angeli as Masséna's statement).
    - Caldiero, side B: 5,700 killed and wounded and no prisoners (Bodart), 5,672 on the 30th
      including 1,800 prisoners (Koch), or 120 officers and 5552 men in all (Angeli).
    - Pojano's prisoners: 1,800 (Bodart) and 1800 (Angeli), or 5,000 (Koch), more than Bodart's
      strength for side B.
  - Castelfranco's date: Koch's page image prints 4 December for Reynier's march on Castel Franco,
    against Bodart's 24 November and Angeli's 24th. Koch's own narrative has Rohan at Bassano on
    the 23rd, and his errata list does not correct the date.
  - Others: how the 30th ended at Caldiero (Gardanne bivouacked on the ground won, Koch; side A
    driven back to Strà, Angeli); whether Verdier's demonstrations kept the Archduke from
    weakening his centre (Koch) or not (Angeli); and Angeli's footnote that the Archduke ordered
    the Verona works left unoccupied, against their occupation in both accounts.
- **Gaps.** The most consequential are side B's strength at Verona, which Bodart does not print;
  side A's strength at Caldiero on 29–31 October; and side B's strength and loss at Pojano and
  Castelfranco. Austrian returns and Hillinger's report in the Kriegsarchiv files Angeli cites, a
  French return of late October, or the Castelfranco capitulation could close them; none was read
  in this pass.
- **Verification.** The four drafts were checked as a campaign before release. The check removed
  OCR-only dates and ordinals from values; added page-image citations for dates and headings that
  values relied on, and Angeli's source notes 75 and 95; and, before registration, rewrote the
  parents' dependency notes to name each note that cites Koch.
- **Usage.** About 2.7 million tokens of the session's budget, roughly 680,000 an entry. The figure
  includes registering two new families (62 records) and learning their page structure. About 7.6
  million remain. At this rate the group's seven remaining entries would need about 4.8 million;
  they need families not yet registered, such as Schönhals and the Correspondance.

## 1805 first pass, batch 1d (2026-10-17)

The Tyrol and Vorarlberg engagements of `third-coalition 1805`, two entries:

| Entry | Engagement | Date (1805) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B368a | Scharnitz, with the Leutasch | 4 November | 23 |
| B368d | Dornbirn | 14 November | 21 |

The main session researched, drafted and verified them without subagents. The work is
primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and two new families ([source additions](source-additions.md)): Moriggl's
  *Der Feldzug des Jahres 1805* (Innsbruck, 1861), the Tyrolean account written from Swinburne's
  papers, the Seefeld diary, Dipauli's notes and survivors' statements, and *Victoires et
  conquêtes*, t. XV (1819), the French compilation. Both are interested accounts. The plan's
  families were checked first and set aside: Angeli's Bd. III and Schönhals only summarise the two
  actions, Alombert and Colin's t. IV gives Scharnitz one sentence, the bulletins in the
  *Correspondance* t. XI say too little, Ney's memoirs end at Ulm, and Mathieu Dumas's volumes for
  1805 are not on the Internet Archive.
  - 52 records were registered, including 24 page images. Every figure in a strength value or a
    recorded loss is read from a page image: Bodart's, through the frame transcription, or one of
    the 24. The drafts cite all 24 figure transcriptions.
  - Moriggl is printed in Fraktur, and its OCR garbles letters and page numbers. The page of each
    quote comes from page marks mapped for pp. 298–439 and checked on the images. The parent repeats
    pp. 348–349 in a second OCR; no quote is taken from that copy.
- **Drafts.** 44 claims with 431 citations. 42 are supported and two are unknown, side A's loss and
  side A's information at Dornbirn; none is disputed. Every draft covers the nine dimensions.
  - Phases: 12 unresolved, 18 inherited, 8 commander-created and 6 post-outcome.
  - Posture: side A attacked at both. At Scharnitz the code is tagged commander-created, since Ney
    decided the attack; at Dornbirn it is tagged inherited, since no passage read shows Augereau or
    Mathieu deciding to attack there.
  - Fortification: side B fought from the fortress of Scharnitz and the Leutasch work, permanent
    works, so the code is tagged inherited. At Dornbirn, Moriggl names entrenchments in the Hohenems
    position and *Victoires et conquêtes* names no works; the two are coded separately, both with
    the phase unresolved.
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - Scharnitz:
    - How the fort fell: three assaults beaten off on 4 November, the garrison's departure in the
      night and Ney's entry into the abandoned fortress (Moriggl), or the 69th's escalade under fire on 5 November
      (*Victoires et conquêtes*), against Bodart's 4 November.
    - The Leutasch garrison: three hundred (*Victoires et conquêtes*), or a battalion given as 600
      with militia companies of 117 and 219 (Moriggl).
    - Side B's prisoners and trophies: eighteen hundred men, a flag and sixteen guns (*Victoires et
      conquêtes*); about 1,500 men, 16 guns and 1 flag (Bodart); no total, twelve guns at Scharnitz
      and a detachment of 1000 prisoners escorted to Kufstein (Moriggl).
    - Side A's loss: about 400 (Bodart); about 36 dead and about 400 wounded, several hundred, 800
      or 1800 in the reports Moriggl quotes; 10 dead and 6 wounded at the Leutasch.
  - Dornbirn:
    - Side A's strength: 15,000 (Bodart), or about 9000 in Wolfskehl's report (Moriggl).
    - The detachment that escaped: about three thousand five hundred men under General
      Wartensleben, fifteen hundred of them cavalry (*Victoires et conquêtes*), or 12 of the 13
      squadrons and 6 guns under the colonels Kinsky and Wartensleben (Moriggl).
    - Where side B's main body stood (Feldkirch or Hohenems), the terms, and the works. Moriggl's
      own text also calls the event the capitulation of Hohenems; that is recorded as deferred,
      since no claim turns on the name.
    - Side B's strength agrees closely: 9 battalions and 4,000 men (Bodart); 3 generals, 160
      officers and 3895 men (Moriggl).
- **Gaps and the targeted follow-up.** The most consequential gap at both is side A's strength,
  which neither narrative gives as a total for the action. The follow-up for both was Moriggl's
  order of battle of the Grande Armée at its crossing of the Rhine (pp. 89, 92 and 93, transcribed
  from the page images; Moriggl names no source for it).
  - Augereau's corps had 16 battalions, 4 squadrons and 14,907 men at the crossing. Bodart's 16
    battalions, 4 squadrons and 15,000 for Dornbirn match it, though the passages read do not show
    whether he drew on Moriggl or on a common source.
  - Loison's and Malcher's divisions, whose brigades the narrative engages at Scharnitz and the
    Leutasch, had 15,615 men together at the crossing (our sum), against Bodart's about 13,000.
  - Both gaps are narrowed, not closed: the table is dated to the crossing, not the engagement, and
    in Moriggl's OCR Ney left part of his forces at Ulm. A return of either corps for November, or
    a report of Ney, Loison, Augereau or Mathieu, could close them; none was read in this pass.
- **Verification.** The two drafts were checked as a campaign before release. The check:
  - removed an OCR-only ordinal from a value;
  - added the page-image and OCR citations that values and renderings relied on, among them Sailer's
    request, the first appearance of the French, and the dates at Dornbirn;
  - corrected three overstatements: the agreement at Landeck, Moriggl's hedged judgment on the
    Hohenems position, and an outpost fight placed "before" Bregenz rather than near it.
- **Usage.** About 2.1 million tokens of the session's budget, roughly 1.05 million an entry. The
  figure includes the search beyond the planned families, registering two new families (52
  records) and mapping Moriggl's Fraktur page marks. About 5.4 million remain. The group's five
  remaining entries would need about 3.4 million at batch 1c's rate and about 5.3 million at this
  batch's; Schönhals, Alombert and Colin's t. IV and the *Correspondance* t. XI are still to be
  registered.

## 1805 first pass, batch 1e (2026-10-17)

The Danube engagements of `third-coalition 1805`, two entries:

| Entry | Engagement | Date (1805) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B368b | Mariazell | 8 November | 20 |
| B368c | Dürnstein | 11 November | 21 |

The main session researched, drafted and verified them without subagents. The work is
primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and two families ([source additions](source-additions.md)):
  - Alombert and Colin's t. IV (1908), the French official history, of the family of tomes II and
    III. The drafts use its narrative and the documents it prints: Davout's report of 9 November,
    the 3rd corps' journal and two corps returns.
  - Schönhals's *Der Krieg 1805 in Deutschland* (1873), the Austrian account written in 1831.

  Both are interested accounts, and they are not fully independent. t. IV follows Schönhals on
  Merveldt's march without citing him. For the evening at Dürrenstein, Schönhals follows the
  Austrian relation that t. IV quotes. Schönhals also takes details from Kotzebue, whom t. IV
  rejects for the fighting.
  - 46 records were registered, including 21 page images. Every figure in a strength value or a
    recorded loss is read from a page image: Bodart's, through the frame transcription, or one of
    the 21. The drafts cite all 21 figure transcriptions; B368c also cites two of Moriggl's (pp. 89
    and 92).
  - t. IV's OCR misreads many figures, above all 1 as 4 (on p. 166, '488' for 155 and '4,600' for
    1,600). Regiment numbers count as ordinals. They stand in a value or rendering only where a
    page image cited in the same claim prints them.
- **Drafts.** 41 claims with 520 citations. 40 are supported and one is unknown, side A's loss at
  Mariazell; none is disputed. Every draft covers the nine dimensions.
  - Phases: 13 unresolved, 11 inherited, 10 commander-created and 7 post-outcome.
  - Posture: side A attacked at Mariazell. At Dürnstein the two accounts are coded separately. In
    Alombert and Colin both sides attacked: Mortier drove Miloradovitch back to Stein, and Dupont
    attacked Doktorow's column. In Schönhals only side B attacked. All three codes are tagged
    commander-created.
  - Fortification: at Mariazell, Schönhals has side B hold Schloss St. Sigmund (B_fortified,
    inherited), and Alombert and Colin name no works (none_named, unresolved). No works are named
    at Dürnstein (none_named, unresolved).
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - Mariazell:
    - Side A's strength: about 15,000 (Bodart). Davout's report and Schönhals have only Heudelet's
      advanced guard engage, with no figure, and in Schönhals Merveldt admits no more than 200
      horse.
    - Side B's loss:
      - 4,000, mostly prisoners, and 16 guns (Bodart);
      - 400 dead, 600 wounded and 4,000 prisoners, 16 guns and 3 flags (Davout);
      - about 4,000 prisoners, 18 guns, 2 flags and 80 vehicles, and 150 more prisoners (Alombert
        and Colin);
      - 2500 men in all, many of whom soon returned (Schönhals).
    - St. Sigmund: a castle held by two companies (Schönhals) or a chapel (Alombert and Colin), and
      with it the fortification code.
    - Merveldt's rank: GM in Bodart, FML in Schönhals.
  - Dürnstein:
    - Side B's strength: 25,000 (Bodart); at least 16,000 assailing Gazan's division (Alombert
      and Colin); 10,000 for the turning force (Kotzebue, through Schönhals).
    - Side A's strength: 8,000 (Bodart). Alombert and Colin give 5,300 for Gazan's division and
      no figure for Dupont's.
    - Side A's loss:
      - 6,600 with 1,600 prisoners (Bodart);
      - about 1,600 to 1,700 with 1,100 to 1,200 prisoners (Alombert and Colin);
      - 980 (the Austrian report XIII-61);
      - 2000 prisoners besides many dead (Schönhals).
    - Side B's loss: 4,000 killed and wounded (Bodart), or 920 (the Austrian report).
    - Dupont's evening fight. In Alombert and Colin, Doktorow was forced to let Mortier pass and
      Dupont's division bivouacked on the field. In Kotzebue, through Schönhals, the division
      re-embarked for Rossatz.
    - Also disputed: the 4e dragons' fate, the trophies, Gazan's escape, the hours of the turning
      column and who set Loiben on fire.
- **Gaps and the targeted follow-ups.** At both engagements the most consequential gap is side
  A's strength.
  - Mariazell: Bodart gives about 15,000, but only an advanced guard of unknown strength
    engaged. The follow-up searched t. IV's appendix of situations for a return of the 3rd
    corps. There is none among the particular situations (pp. 748–770). The list of the Grande
    Armée at 6 brumaire (28 October), read from the page images of pp. 711, 723 and 724, gives
    the 13e légère and the 108e 2 battalions each but no men. The gap is narrowed to the advanced
    guard's composition, not closed.
  - Dürnstein: no strength for Dupont's division. The follow-up read the 6th corps' return of 4
    brumaire (p. 769), which records the division as detached with Murat and gives it no
    figures. Moriggl's order of battle, already registered, gives it 7 battalions and 5350 men at
    the Rhine crossing. The gap stays open.
  - Sources that could close them, none read: a return of the 3rd or 6th corps for November,
    Heudelet's detailed report, the journal of Dupont's division, or Alombert's book of 1897.
- **A lead for the group check.** t. IV's p. 770 was viewed while searching the situations and
  was not registered. It is the 7th corps' approximate return of 4 brumaire an XIV (26
  October), dated at Fribourg and signed by Donzelot.
  - As read from the image: 491 officers and 10,753 men present under arms, 11,244 in all, and a
    total force of 12,606. The column sums check (our arithmetic).
  - It bears on side A's strength at Dornbirn. B368d has Bodart's 15,000, Moriggl's 14,907 at the
    Rhine crossing and about 9000 in Wolfskehl's report.
  - It has not been added to B368d, whose first pass made its one follow-up.
- **Verification.** The two drafts were checked as a campaign before release. The check:
  - re-read in full every OCR section cited, and checked every page locator against the running
    heads;
  - removed OCR-only figures from values, among them dates and regiment numbers, and added the
    page-image citations (pp. 166 and 769) that the values relied on;
  - extended quotes that values went beyond;
  - corrected two misreadings: the corps quarters at Dürrenstein were Mortier's, not Gazan's, and
    the allies held their belief "for some time", not "long";
  - fixed a capturing-group bug in the scratch check for OCR digits, which before this batch had
    reported no digits; rerun on every committed draft of the group, it found nothing to
    correct;
  - completed the parents' inspection records from the session's commands. This corrected two
    statements of what had been read: the rest of t. IV's 10 November section was read only
    around keyword hits, and its 8 November section starts on p. 117.
- **Usage.** About 2.3 million tokens of the session's budget, roughly 1.15 million an entry. The
  figure includes registering two new families (46 records) and the two follow-ups through t.
  IV's appendix. About 3.1 million remain. The group's three remaining entries (Ober-Hollabrunn,
  Austerlitz and Steken) would need about 3.2 million at batch 1d's rate and about 3.5 million at
  this batch's, more than remains, and Austerlitz may need more than an average entry.

## 1805 first pass, batch 1f (2026-10-17)

The last three engagements of `third-coalition 1805`:

| Entry | Engagement | Date (1805) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B369a | Ober-Hollabrunn | 16 November | 21 |
| B369c | Austerlitz | 2 December | 21 |
| B370a | Steken | 5 December | 20 |

All 22 of the group's entries are now drafted. The main session researched, drafted and verified
these three without subagents. The work is primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and two families for each entry ([source additions](source-additions.md)):
  - Ober-Hollabrunn and Austerlitz: *Victoires et conquêtes*, t. XV (1819), the French
    compilation registered in batch 1d, and Schönhals (1873), the Austrian account registered in
    batch 1e.
  - Steken: Schönhals and a new family, Völderndorff's *Kriegsgeschichte von Bayern* (1826). It is
    the Bavarian history, written on higher orders from the archives and the commanders' reports,
    Wrede's communications among them. Side A at Steken is Wrede's Bavarian corps. The other
    Bavarian candidate, Heilmann's *Wrede* (1881, Internet Archive `11746375bsb`), was found and
    not used: the brief allows two families besides Bodart.

  The three are interested accounts of their own sides. By its preface Schönhals draws what it
  says of the French army's operations from that army's bulletins, and *Victoires et conquêtes*
  cites the official bulletin in its Hollabrunn section, so for side A the two may share the
  bulletins. At Austerlitz Schönhals cites Dumas for the French strength and loss. No passage read
  shows dependence between Völderndorff and Schönhals.
  - 55 records were registered: the Völderndorff parent, three selections, 25 page images and 26
    figure transcriptions. One transcription is a second one of t. XV's p. 215, whose image batch
    1d registered, for the Hollabrunn passage at the top of the page.
  - Every figure in a strength value or a recorded loss is read from a page image: Bodart's,
    through the frame transcription, or one of the figure transcriptions. The drafts cite 27: the
    26 new ones and Schönhals's p. 137 from batch 1e.
  - The OCR misreads figures the images print: Schönhals's '16.000' for 15.000 on p. 169, and in
    Völderndorff '30 Kanonen' for 40 (p. 296), '50,000' for 30,000 (p. 299), and '291 Mann' for
    791 and '750 Mann' for 756 (p. 301). Schönhals's order of battle of the allied army (pp.
    177–178) is garbled in the OCR, and the drafts cite it only from the page images.
  - t. XV's leaf numbers shift by two between pp. 226 and 227 (leaves n255 and n258), so every
    image's printed page was checked on the image.
- **Drafts.** 62 claims with 561 citations, all supported; none is unknown or disputed. Every
  draft covers the nine dimensions.
  - Phases: 23 commander-created, 15 inherited, 12 unresolved and 12 post-outcome.
  - Posture: side A attacked at Ober-Hollabrunn, where Murat attacked Bagration's position behind
    Schöngrabern. Side B attacked at Steken, where Archduke Ferdinand attacked Wrede's positions at
    Stecken and Wonau. Both sides attacked at Austerlitz: the allies against the French right at
    Telnitz and Sokolnitz, the French on the Pratzen heights. All three codes are tagged
    commander-created.
  - Fortification: at Austerlitz side A fortified the santon, the height on which Lannes's left
    rested and which Napoleon regarded as the key of his offensive operations (A_fortified,
    commander-created). Neither account names works at Ober-Hollabrunn or Steken (none_named,
    unresolved).
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - Ober-Hollabrunn:
    - Side A's strength: 30,000 (Bodart); twenty-two thousand who attacked (*Victoires*); no
      figure for the force engaged (Schönhals).
    - Side B: 7,000 Russians and Austrians (Bodart, his infantry and cavalry summed, our
      arithmetic); six thousand Russians, the Austrians having been allowed to separate before
      the fight (*Victoires*, OCR); 6000 Russians, with the Hessen-Homburg hussars of Nostitz's
      brigade in the fight (Schönhals).
    - The deceptions before the fight, side B's prisoners and side A's loss (about 2,000 in Bodart,
      considerable in Schönhals, no number in *Victoires*).
  - Austerlitz:
    - Side B's strength: 83,000 (Bodart); from more than eighty thousand to nearly a hundred
      thousand (*Victoires*); nearly 89.000 on 25 November and 82575 in the order of battle
      (Schönhals). The Austrians: 16,000 (Bodart) or about eighteen thousand (*Victoires*).
    - Side A's loss: 10,000 killed and wounded (Bodart); two thousand dead and four or five
      thousand wounded (*Victoires*); 800 dead and 6000 wounded (Dumas, through Schönhals).
    - Side B's loss: 36,000 with 20,000 prisoners (Bodart); more than forty thousand
      (*Victoires*); 5922 Austrians, about 15.000 Russian prisoners and 5000 to 6000 Russian dead
      and wounded (Schönhals).
    - Also disputed: the drowning in the retreat, Oudinot's grenadiers (Schönhals's pp. 167 and
      169) and Valhubert's death (t. XV's pp. 267 and 271).
  - Steken:
    - Side B's strength: 11,000 (Bodart); 8294 after the junction at Czaslau (Schönhals); 17
      battalions, 2,000 horse and 40 guns, and an enemy army of 30,000 (Völderndorff). Bodart's
      battalions, horse and guns are Völderndorff's.
    - Side B's loss: 800 (Bodart); 756 by the enemy's admission (Völderndorff); 205 (Schönhals's
      dead, wounded and missing, our arithmetic).
    - Side A's loss: about 1,000 (Bodart); 130 officers, 791 men and 56 horses (Völderndorff); 20
      officers and about 1000 men taken, besides dead and wounded (Schönhals).
    - Also disputed: the Austrian wing commanders, the days before the action, Wrede's rank and
      the manner of side A's retreat.
- **Gaps and the targeted follow-ups.**
  - Ober-Hollabrunn: side A's engaged strength. The follow-up read the passages around every hit
    for Hollabrunn, Schöngrabern and Guntersdorf in both parents and found no other figure. A
    return of the 4th and 5th corps and the cavalry reserve for mid-November could close it.
  - Austerlitz: side B's strength. The follow-up registered Schönhals's order of battle from its
    page images. Its recapitulation sums to 114 battalions, 10 companies, 170¾ squadrons, 82575
    men and 16565 horses. Bodart's 114 battalions and 83,000 agree with it (82575 rounded up, our
    arithmetic), but his 148 squadrons and 16,000 cavalry are not the table's. The table gives no
    guns, and its 3rd column's rows sum to 16 battalions against the recapitulation's 18. It does
    not separate the Austrians, so Bodart's 16,000 Austrians stay unchecked; the enclosure on the
    Austrian troops that Schönhals's p. 156 names could close that.
  - Steken: side A's strength on 5 December. Völderndorff gives Wrede 4,500 foot, barely 400
    serviceable horses and six guns on 29 November, and lists the units posted on the 5th without
    a total. The follow-up searched his OCR from the middle of p. 250 to the top of p. 283 for
    figures of men, horses, battalions, squadrons and guns and found none for Wrede's corps.
    Wrede's returns for early December or Schönhals's Beilage 37 could narrow both sides'
    strengths.
- **Leads for the group check.** The Steken follow-up found two brief accounts in Völderndorff
  outside that entry, read and not cited: Dürrenstein (p. 275 by the OCR page heads), with
  strengths for Gazan's division and the Russian army, and Hollabrunn (pp. 278–279), with a
  strength for Bagration's rearguard. They bear on B368c and B369a. With t. IV's p. 770 (batch
  1e's lead for B368d), they are leads, not additions to the drafts.
- **Verification.** The three drafts were checked as a campaign before release. The check:
  - read every cited passage, and checked each page locator against the OCR's running heads or
    page numbers;
  - checked every figure in a value against a page-image quote of the same claim, with a scratch
    check whose joiner was fixed first (it had merged digit groups across quotes); its one
    remaining flag, B369a's 7,000, is labelled our arithmetic;
  - replaced OCR-only figures in values and renderings with "(OCR)" markers, among them a
    distance at Ober-Hollabrunn and a column number at Steken;
  - corrected a page in the record of the Steken follow-up: Völderndorff's Hollabrunn account is
    on pp. 278–279 by the OCR page heads.
- **Usage.** About 2.8 million tokens of the session's budget, roughly 930,000 an entry,
  including a new family, 55 records and the order of battle. The group's 22 entries used about
  14.6 million, roughly 660,000 an entry. The cohort's other 15 groups hold 303 engagements: about
  200 million at the group's average rate, between about 115 million at batch 1b's rate and about
  350 million at batch 1e's. At about 15 million a session that is roughly 14 sessions, 8 to 24
  over the range. About 0.3 million remain in this session.

## 1805 group check (2026-10-17)

After batch 1f the main session checked the 22 drafts of `third-coalition 1805` together. The
check was bounded and is primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Scope.** Two scratch checks, run over every draft and kept outside the repository:
  - every digit in a claim's value against the page-image quotes of the same claim (Bodart's
    transcription and the figure transcriptions);
  - every English rendering marked as OCR that holds a digit or a number word.

  The passages behind each flag that bears on a strength, a loss or a date were read. Out of
  scope: re-reading the cited passages of batches 1a–1e, which each batch's own verification did,
  and the leads that batches 1e and 1f recorded.
- **Fixed.**
  - B364a (Parsdorf). Three claims said the Bavarian corps' journal dates the pursuit and the
    fight near Parsdorf to 20 vendémiaire (12 October). On the page images of t. III vol. 2 (pp.
    661–662), the paragraphs on the entry into Munich and the pursuit carry no date. They are
    headed by subject and follow the journal's 21 vendémiaire (13 October) entry. The claims now
    say so. Claim `date-of-captures` cites Bernadotte's letter dated at Munich on 20 vendémiaire
    at 11.30 in the evening, which reports the pursuit, and the 12 October date is marked as our
    reading.
  - B364a also stated two figures without quoting them: the 240 horses of Wrede's pursuit and the
    journal's 1,100 prisoners. The claims that state them now quote them from the page image.
  - B364c (Memmingen). Claim `side-b-losses` stated Bodart's 11 battalions and 6,000 men without
    citing him. The citation is added.
  - No status, phase or code changed. The group holds 465 claims, 4 of them unknown, with 3,294
    citations, 6 more than before the check.
- **No change after reading.** The 82 renderings flagged by the second check hold:
  - page numbers;
  - the pronoun 'one' and the adverb 'first';
  - batch 1c's marked OCR figures, written as '1 (OCR)' rather than '... (OCR)';
  - regiment numbers in B368c, each printed on a page image cited in the same claim.
- **Recorded, not changed: each draft keeps the standard of its batch.** The rule for figures seen
  only in the OCR tightened during the group. Batches 1a and 1b kept OCR-only dates and ordinals
  in values. Batch 1c removed dates and ordinals, and batch 1e added regiment numbers. The first
  check, run after the fixes, leaves entry IDs aside and finds two kinds of value digits:
  - 67 that occur only in an OCR quote of the same claim: 41 in batch 1a, 18 in 1b and 8 in 1c.
    They are dates, days of the month, and corps, division and regiment numbers. Batch 1c's 8 are
    days of the battle of Caldiero, which Bodart's entry prints.
  - 105 that occur in no quote of the same claim: 49, 23, 21, 9, 2 and 1 in batches 1a–1f. They
    include sums labelled as ours, a date converted from the Republican calendar, and two French
    idioms read as ranges ('de 12 à 1,500 mètres' and '3 à 400 prisonniers'). The rest are dates
    and ordinals taken from the context or from headings quoted in other claims.

  Each of the 67 was read in context, and none looks implausible against the events. None was
  checked against a page image. Bringing batches 1a–1c to the later rule would mean removing or
  re-citing these tokens. That would change no model input and is not done here.
- **Verification.** The evidence check passes on all 2,237 sources and 22 drafts. The packet was
  regenerated, `make reproduce` was run, and `make check` passes.

## 1806 first pass: Naples (2026-10-17)

The four engagements of `third-coalition 1806`, the French conquest of the kingdom of Naples:

| Entry | Engagement | Dates (Bodart) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B370c | Campo Tenese | 9 March 1806 | 18 |
| B370d | Maida | 4 July 1806 | 21 |
| B371a | Siege of Gaëta | 4 March to 18 July 1806 | 20 |
| B379c | Siege of Amantea | 18 December 1806 to 6 February 1807 | 19 |

The frame's group also holds B370b, the naval action of 6 February 1806, which cohort v1 leaves
out because it keeps land entries only. The main session researched, drafted and verified the four
without subagents. The work is primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart, Koch and one further family for each entry
  ([source additions](source-additions.md)):
  - Koch's *Mémoires de Masséna*, t. V (1850), whose parent batch 1c registered, for all four. It
    is the French history from Masséna's papers and the war depot, and second-hand for side B.
  - Campo Tenese: the *Memoirs of the Comte Roger de Damas* (1913 English translation), the
    memoirs of side B's commander, with the editor's notes.
  - Maida: Bunbury's *Narratives* (1854), by the British army's Quartermaster-General in the
    Mediterranean, who rode the field. By its preface it was written many years after the events,
    and by the narrative his French figures rest on his memory of returns he held and lost.
  - Gaëta and Amantea: Colletta's *Storia del Reame di Napoli*, vol. II (1856), the history of the
    kingdom by a Neapolitan officer. The passages read give him no part in either siege and name
    no source.

  Damas, Bunbury and Colletta are new families. 127 records were registered: three OCR parents,
  four selections, and 60 page images with their figure transcriptions. Every figure in a strength
  value or a recorded loss is read from a page image: Bodart's, through the frame transcription, or
  one of the figure transcriptions. Sums are labelled as ours.
  - Bodart's 4,800 for side B at Maida is Bunbury's 4795 rank and file rounded up, and his 4,700 at
    Amantea equals Koch's three bodies (our arithmetic). His 3,000, 12,000 and 7,000 at Gaëta
    match figures in Koch, but the passages read do not show what he used.
  - No passage read shows dependence between Koch and Damas, Bunbury or Colletta.
  - Koch's OCR of his pièces justificatives and of Verdier's report on Amantea reads figures
    differently from his text, and no figure is taken from it. Koch's p. 282 is heavily inked: its
    transcription reads the first figure of '22' and of '48' by their shapes.
- **Drafts.** 78 claims with 715 citations: 76 supported and 2 unknown, side B's losses and its
  information at Amantea. Every draft covers the nine dimensions.
  - Phases: 27 inherited, 23 unresolved, 15 commander-created and 13 post-outcome.
  - Posture:
    - Campo Tenese: side A attacked (A_attacked). The posture is tagged inherited, because the
      council presided by Joseph set the attack on Damas's corps.
    - Maida: the accounts differ, so there are two claims, both commander-created. In Koch the
      French attacked a British line formed to receive them (A_attacked). In Bunbury both armies
      advanced to attack (both_attacked).
    - Gaëta and Amantea: side A besieged (A_attacked, inherited). At Gaëta the king and his council
      set the bombardment and the pace of the siege. At Amantea the siege carried out the marshal's
      orders.
  - Fortification:
    - Campo Tenese: side B fought from the redoubts Damas says he made (B_fortified,
      commander-created).
    - Maida: neither account names works that either side fought from (none_named, unresolved).
    - The two sieges: side B held permanent walls, and side A fought from its trenches and
      batteries (both_fortified). The code is tagged unresolved, because the permanent works are
      inherited and the siege works commander-created. A coding that counted only the defended
      position would give B_fortified.
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - Campo Tenese:
    - Side B's strength: 10,000 (Bodart); about 12,000 for Damas's corps and about 18,000 for both
      corps, whose parts make about 20,000 (Koch); twelve thousand for Damas's army corps, which his
      editor thinks excludes Rosenheim's (Damas).
    - Side B's command: two corps, under Rosenheim and Damas (Koch); one army corps of two wings
      under Damas (Damas).
    - Side B's loss: 3,000, mostly prisoners, with 2 generals, 100 officers and all the guns
      (Bodart); 1,900 prisoners with two brigadier generals, 100 officers and a battalion of the
      guard, and the artillery (Koch); about 2000 prisoners, more than 100 of them officers
      (Damas's editor, who adds Marulli's 250 without saying what it counts).
    - The location: in Basilicata south of Lauria (Bodart); on the outskirts of Calabria between
      the border and Morano (Damas's editor).
  - Maida:
    - The date: 4 July (Bodart and Bunbury); the 5th (Koch).
    - Side A's strength: 4,300 (Bodart's infantry and cavalry); 4,800 (Koch); about 6300 in action
      of 7300 under command (Bunbury, from memory). The first two sums are ours.
    - Side B's strength: 4,800 English and Neapolitan infantry (Bodart); 7,000 English and 3,000
      Neapolitan or Sicilian troops embarked (Koch); 4795 rank and file marching and about 5300 in
      the field, with no Neapolitan or Sicilian unit named (Bunbury).
    - Side A's loss: 1,700 with 1 general and 55 officers (Bodart); 1,000 killed and wounded in
      the Swiss regiment (Koch); more than 500 French dead buried (Bunbury). Side B's loss: 350
      (Bodart); 326 killed and wounded (Bunbury, our arithmetic).
    - The posture, and the British position: on the beach with their backs to the sea (Koch); in
      the plain after a march along the shore (Bunbury).
  - Gaëta:
    - Side A's strength at the end: 12,000 (Bodart); about twelve thousand, beside a state of the
      siege troops totalling 12,016 (Koch); fourteen thousand (Colletta). Its artillery: 94 guns
      (Bodart); 90 pieces and a mobile battery of 6 (Koch); eighty heavy guns and mortars
      (Colletta).
    - Side B's strength: 4,000 at first and 7,000 at the end (Bodart); fewer than 6,000 before the
      siege, about 6,200 by deserters' reports in early July and 7,000 in his reflections (Koch);
      about seven thousand on 7 July and three thousand four hundred prisoners at the surrender
      (Colletta).
    - The French loss: about 1,000 with 86 officers (Bodart); 819 with 29 officers (Koch); eleven
      hundred (Colletta). The officer total and Koch's figures are our sums. The Neapolitan loss:
      about 1,000 (Bodart); about equal to the besiegers', without 152 deserters (Koch); nine
      hundred (Colletta).
    - Side A's commander: Masséna for the whole siege (Bodart); Lacour, then Gardanne from 17 May,
      with the marshal at Castellone only at the end (Koch).
    - Also disputed: the ships on 7 July, the rounds fired, the parole, the name of the governor's
      successor, why the prince defended the place, and the morning of the 18th.
  - Amantea:
    - Side A's commander: Reynier (Bodart and Koch); Verdier (Colletta, who does not name
      Reynier).
    - Side B's strength: about 1,300 (Bodart), a figure not in the passages read; regular troops,
      some hundreds of insurgents and the inhabitants (Koch); few soldiers and many Bourbon
      partisans (Colletta).
    - Side B's guns: 4 to 5 pieces (Koch); three iron cannon (Colletta).
    - The siege's length: 33 days (Koch); forty, without the first assault (Colletta).
    - Also disputed: the distance from Cosenza, the parole, the honours of war, the cause of the
      surrender, and the strength of Verdier's first attempt in December, before Bodart's dates
      (1,300 in Koch, three thousand two hundred in Colletta).
- **Gaps and the targeted follow-ups.**
  - Campo Tenese: the strengths engaged on 9 March. The follow-up read Koch's pièce justificative
    n° III (pp. 401–403) in the parent OCR, the army's composition at its reorganisation. It lists
    Reynier's corps by regiment, but its figures are garbled in the OCR and it is a state before
    the march, so it was not registered.
  - Maida: side A's strength and loss. The follow-up read the headings and tables of Koch's pièces
    II–XIV (pp. 400–428) for a return of Reynier's division in July and found none.
  - Gaëta: side B's strength. The follow-up searched the OCR of Koch's siege chapter for any other
    statement of the garrison's strength and found none. Pièce n° XI, to which Koch refers for his
    comparison of besiegers and defenders, is a note on King Joseph's guard.
  - Amantea: side B's strength. The follow-up searched all of Koch's OCR for Amantea and Mirabelli
    and found no strength for the garrison. It found only Koch's remark that Amantea and other
    places were simple coast batteries, not fortresses.

  A Neapolitan or British return could narrow the two garrisons' strengths, and a French return of
  early July the strength at Maida.
- **Verification.** The four drafts were checked as a campaign before release. The check:
  - read every cited passage, and checked each page locator against the page images or the OCR's
    page heads;
  - checked every figure in a value against a page-image quote of the same claim, and every
    English rendering of an OCR passage for digits and number words, with the 1805 group check's
    scratch checks; the remaining flags are page numbers and the pronoun 'one';
  - removed a citation that took Gaëta past the brief's ceiling. B371a cited one passage of
    Bunbury (p. 235), read for Maida, as a fourth family beside Bodart, Koch and Colletta. The
    claim that cited it, `side-b-objectives`, now rests on Koch and Colletta.
  - found every figure transcription cited. Of the selections' 41 sections, 26 are cited. The
    other 15 are 5 passages behind the source records' provenance notes and 10 OCR passages whose
    pages are cited from their images. The 5 are Bunbury's preface, a passage of the introduction
    to Damas's memoirs, the account of his manuscripts and his own note at their head, and
    Colletta's entry in his volume's index.

  The evidence check passes on all 2,364 sources and 26 drafts (543 claims, 6 unknown). The packet
  was regenerated, `make reproduce` was run, and `make check` passes.
- **Usage.** About 2.3 million tokens of the session's budget, roughly 580,000 an entry, including
  three new families and 127 records. The cohort's other 14 groups hold 299 engagements: about 175
  million at this group's rate and about 200 million at the 1805 group's. At about 15 million a
  session that is roughly 12 to 13 sessions. About 12.5 million remain in this session.

## 1806 first pass, batch 2a: Saalfeld, Jena and Auerstedt (2026-10-18)

The first batch of `fourth-coalition 1806` (23 entries), the war against Prussia and Saxony, from
Saalfeld to the two battles of 14 October:

| Entry | Engagement | Dates (Bodart) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B371b | Saalfeld | 10 October 1806 | 22 |
| B372a | Jena | 14 October 1806 | 21 |
| B373a | Auerstedt | 14 October 1806 | 22 |

The group's five batches are planned in the raw directory's `NOTES.md`. The main session researched,
drafted and verified the three without subagents. The work is primary-verified; no separate review
was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and two new families, one for each side
  ([source additions](source-additions.md)):
  - Foucart's *Campagne de Prusse (1806): Iéna* (1887), the French staff edition of documents from
