Groß-Glogau, the Prussian fortress on the Oder in Silesia.

| Entry | Engagement | Date (Bodart) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B377b | Groß-Glogau (investment and capitulation) | 2 December 1806 | 18 |

The main session researched, drafted and verified the entry without subagents. The work is
primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and the group's two families. Every cited passage was read from a registered
  page image ([source additions](source-additions.md)):
  - Höpfner, Theil 2, Bd. 4 (1851), a new volume of his family: the Glogau account of
    chapter 15, pp. 15–31, each page transcribed whole. The chapter opens the volume with the
    first measures for the defence of Silesia; that part was not read beyond its heading and
    first page. He gives the Confederation of the Rhine's corps in Silesia unit by unit and the
    garrison by its parts, naming no source for either. He marks the governor's answer of
    13 November as given by French statements, and prints in French footnotes the governor's
    letter of refusal and Berthier's answer to Jérôme's report on the abandoned storm.
  - Foucart's *Campagne de Pologne*, t. I:
    - Davout's letter to the Emperor of 4 November (pp. 41–42);
    - the major general's letters to Prince Jérôme of 5, 16 and 22 November (pp. 56–57, 104–105
      and 145);
    - the Emperor's letter to Jérôme of 10 November (p. 81);
    - Foucart's narrative of 7 November (p. 68);
    - reused from sub-batch 2c-iv: the 33rd Bulletin with the suspension of arms of 16 November,
      whose article 2 names Glogau, the 35th Bulletin (pp. 132–133) and the Emperor's letter of
      3 December (pp. 215–216).
  - Foucart t. II, registered earlier: the 30th and 32nd Bulletins (p. 806; pp. 848–849).

  The French documents were found by an OCR search of *Campagne de Pologne* t. I for Glogau
  (35 lines). 53 records were registered: the Höpfner Bd. 4 OCR parent and 26 page images (17 of
  Höpfner and 9 of *Campagne de Pologne*) with their figure transcriptions. No figure in a value is
  taken from the OCR.
- **Not used: Völderndorff.** The Bavarian official history, *Kriegsgeschichte von Bayern* (1826),
  registered for Steken in 1805, tells the Bavarian divisions' part at Glogau in its fourth book.
  Its pp. 56–58, 61–62 and 64–66 were read on the page images and transcribed, and the first draft
  cited them as a fourth family. The check before release removed them: the brief allows two
  families besides Bodart, and the group's two campaign families were kept, as Heilmann's *Wrede*
  was left out at Steken. The 14 page records were withdrawn before commit, so no Völderndorff
  figure or passage stands in the draft.
- **Draft.** 18 claims with 262 citations, all supported. Phases: 10 unresolved, 3 inherited,
  3 post_outcome and 2 commander_created.
  - The posture is coded `A_attacked` and left unresolved. Side A invested, bombarded and
    summoned the fortress. Side B stopped all sorties that could give occasion for desertion,
    apart from a raid on the Grundmühle and a move into the outworks against side A's pits.
  - The fortification is coded `both_fortified` and left unresolved: side B's permanent fortress,
    and side A's gun cover, trenches and pits. Höpfner also says that side A opened no siege works;
    a coding that counted only the fortress would give `B_fortified`.
  - Side A's losses are a partial post_outcome claim: the Grundmühle post of 1 officer and 23 men
    taken on 18 November, and no loss at battery No. 4 on 16 November. No total is given.
  - Bodart's commander, Deroy, left with his division on 25 November, and Vandamme, with the
    Württemberg division, took the capitulation. The draft assigns the credit to no one commander.
  - The boundary leaves the cavalry's move on Breslau from 14 November, and the siege of Breslau,
    to Bodart's Breslau entry. The suspension of arms of 16 November, never ratified, is context.
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - The garrison: 3228 men and 124 guns (Höpfner); 3,200 men and 124 guns (Bodart), so Bodart's
    figure is not Höpfner's rounded up; 3,500, of them 1,000 cavalry (Davout's officer,
    4 November); one regiment (the 32nd Bulletin).
  - Side A at the capitulation: 9,000, of them 1,000 French, under Deroy (Bodart); the Württemberg
    division of about 8000 under Vandamme, after Deroy's division had left (Höpfner and the order
    of 22 November). No passage read names a French troop unit before Glogau.
  - Höpfner's own table: the second Bavarian division's rows make 7018, not the printed 5830, the
    Württemberg rows 8652, not 8642, and the corps' arms 21,946, not 22,946, while the printed
    division totals make 22,657 (our arithmetic).
  - The first force before Glogau: Lefebvre's brigade and a light battery (Höpfner); 3 cavalry
    regiments and 6 pieces (Foucart's narrative); 2,000 Bavarian horse (the 30th Bulletin), where
    Höpfner's table gives the three regiments 1097 men (our arithmetic).
  - Deroy's investment: the 10th (Höpfner); the 9th (the 30th Bulletin). The first bombardment:
    on the 8th by 10 howitzers (the 30th Bulletin); a cannonade on the 7th and 8th and the first
    fire from six batteries on the 13th (Höpfner).
  - The siege guns: 6 mortars and 4 howitzers ordered (the Emperor, 10 November); 6 small and
    4 heavy mortars and 4 heavy howitzers disembarked (Höpfner).
  - The governor's answer of 13 November: that he recognised the impossibility of defence, by
    French statements (Höpfner's text); a refusal (the letter in Höpfner's footnote).
  - The siege works: none opened (Höpfner's verdict); batteries, trenches and pits (his
    narrative).
  - The date: 2 December (Bodart); agreed on the 2nd and handed over on the 3rd (Höpfner). The
    prisoners: the garrison captive, its officers on parole (Bodart); the invalids released and
    the rest prisoners (Höpfner). Bodart's 15-day blockade counts back to 17 November, while
    Höpfner has the place invested on 7 November (our arithmetic).
- **Gaps.** No targeted follow-up was run.
  - The most consequential gap is side A's strength and composition at the capitulation. A return
    of the Württemberg division or Vandamme's report could close it.
  - Deferred:
    - Höpfner's Plan Nr. XVII of Glogau, and the part of his chapter 15 before p. 15;
    - *Campagne de Pologne* p. 146, the rest of the order of 22 November, kept for Breslau;
    - not found in the pages read: the capitulation's text, the royal order of 2 November,
      Berthier's letter on the storm (seen only in Höpfner's footnote) and Jérôme's and
      Lefebvre's reports;
    - the contemporary account of the siege of Breslau in the Lower Silesian Digital Library,
      kept for Breslau.
  - The passages read do not show whether the governor or Jérôme knew of the suspension of arms.
- **Verification.** The pages and the draft were checked before release. The check:
  - transcribed Höpfner's pages whole and the Foucart passages from the page images, every figure
    on full-resolution crops, and corrected the transcription notes against the images before
    registration, among them the word divided as 'Dek-' and 'kungen' across pp. 23–24, the print's
    spelling 'Desnöettes' on Foucart's p. 68, and the running head of p. 81;
  - reread every cited passage against its transcription and fixed what the reread found, among it:
    - side A's third summons of 13 November followed its withdrawal of the guns behind infantry
      pickets after the fire; the draft now cites that sentence;
    - Lefebvre's two squadrons crossed at Neusalz so that the fortress could be invested on both
      banks, not to invest the right bank alone;
    - on 30 November the wet roads held back the small mortars and the howitzers, brought up on
      sledges, until 8 in the evening; the draft no longer says that all the guns had to come on
      sledges;
    - the capitulation was agreed after Vandamme's staff officer brought proposals; the draft no
      longer says it was agreed with Vandamme in person;
    - Höpfner's printed division totals, 14,015 and 8642, make 22,657 (our arithmetic), another
      discrepancy now in the strength claim;
  - cited transcribed passages that the first draft had left out: the corps' arrival at Krossen,
    the spoiled muskets and cartridges in Tschepe's battalion, and the Lissa detachment ordered on
    16 November;
  - corrected the new parent record's inspection note: chapter 15 begins the volume's text, not
    p. 15, and turns from Glogau to Breslau on p. 31;
  - removed the fourth family, as above.

  The evidence check passes on all 2,910 sources and 45 drafts (898 claims, 15 unknown). The packet
  was regenerated, `make reproduce` was run, and `make check` passes (239 tests).
- **Usage.** About 2.3 million tokens by the session's token counter, including the Völderndorff
  pages read and withdrawn. The estimate for the cohort's other 280 engagements stays at about 165
  to 190 million tokens.

## 1806 first pass, sub-batch 2d-ii: Breslau (2026-10-18)

The second part of batch 2d of `fourth-coalition 1806`: the siege and capitulation of Breslau,
the capital of Prussian Silesia. Batch 2d is complete.

| Entry | Engagement | Date (Bodart) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B378b | Breslau (siege and capture) | 6 December 1806 to 6 January 1807 | 18 |

The main session researched, drafted and verified the entry without subagents. The work is
primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and the group's two families. Every cited passage was read from a registered
  page image ([source additions](source-additions.md)):
  - Höpfner, Bd. 4, chapter 16, each page transcribed whole: the King's order of 22 November to
    the commandants (p. 36); the defence's preparations, the second investment and the siege to
    the failed storm of 23 December (pp. 47–65); the summons, the armistice and its end on
    25–26 December (pp. 79–81); the governor's conduct on 30 December, while the relief was beaten
    nearby (pp. 86–87); and the end of the siege, the capitulation, the governor's reasons and
    Höpfner's judgement (pp. 109–112, 114, 116 and 118). His formation table of the Silesian corps
    (pp. 15–17, registered for Groß-Glogau) is reused. He cites General Lindener's journal,
    Colonel Blein's report to Napoleon, a statement of the French artillery officers, Du Casse and
    the *Geschichte der Belagerung von Breslau*, and names no source for the garrison's returns,
    the councils or the negotiations.
  - Foucart's *Campagne de Pologne*, t. II (1882), newly registered: the Emperor's letters to
    Prince Jérôme of 3 December (pp. 93–94) and to the major general of 17 December (p. 97) and
    7 January (p. 113); the major general's letter to Jérôme of 3 December (p. 94) and Jérôme's
    letter to Vandamme of 8 December (p. 96), both as Foucart summarises and excerpts them; and
    Foucart's narrative of the investment, the covering detachments and the capitulation
    (pp. 93, 95–97, 108 and 112). By its own footnote it leaves the operations against the place
    aside.

  The pages were found by OCR scans of Höpfner's chapter 16 and of t. II for Breslau. 81 records
  were registered: the t. II OCR parent and 40 page images (32 of Höpfner and 8 of t. II) with their
  figure transcriptions. Every registered page is cited. No figure in a value is taken from the
  OCR.
- **Not used.** Völderndorff's Bavarian history and the contemporary account of the siege in the
  Lower Silesian Digital Library, both noted for this entry, were not read: the brief allows two
  families besides Bodart. *Campagne de Pologne* t. I pp. 146 and 242 were read in the OCR and not
  used.
- **Draft.** 18 claims with 426 citations, all supported. Phases: 10 unresolved, 3 inherited,
  3 post_outcome and 2 commander_created.
  - The posture is coded `A_attacked` and left unresolved. Side A invested, bombarded, summoned
    and tried to storm the fortress. Side B burnt the suburbs that covered side A, made the
    expedition of 7 December and the sortie of 16 December, repulsed the storm and did not sortie
    on 30 December.
  - The fortification is coded `both_fortified` and left unresolved: side B's permanent fortress
    and side A's parallels, approaches and batteries. A coding that counted only the fortress would
    give `B_fortified`.
  - Side A's losses are a partial post_outcome claim: Captain Debruir captured, 10 prisoners taken
    by the sortie, losses without figures in the night to 23 December and in the storm, where two
    raft carriers were shot and a French officer and some privates captured. No total is given.
  - The command is left open from 20 December. The Emperor's order of 17 December left the
    command of the siege to Vandamme; by Höpfner, Jérôme kept the corps command through
    Hédouville, and Vandamme acted without Hédouville's knowledge. The draft assigns the credit to
    no one commander.
  - The boundary leaves the Prussian relief attempts under the Prince of Anhalt-Pless, among them
    Strehlen and the advance of 30 December, and the French operations against them outside the
    entry. Bodart has no entry for them.
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - The garrison: 6,000 men with 272 guns (Bodart); 135 officers and 5841 men on 1 January and
    6000 at the second investment, of whom Höpfner counts 5000 as real defenders against the
    10,320 of the arming plan of 1805, with 208 guns on the walls and 46 in reserve or unusable;
    a fifth of the garrison necessary (the Emperor, from intercepted letters). Bodart's 6,000 and
    135 officers are Höpfner's figures, but his 272 guns match none: 208 and 46 make 254 (our
    arithmetic).
  - The garrison at the end: 116 officers and 5270 men marched out, 240 guns handed over
    (Höpfner); 5,500 men filed past (Foucart); 135 officers on parole (Bodart). The marchers are
    19 officers and 571 men fewer than the return of 1 January (our arithmetic).
  - Side A: 27,000 with 52 guns (Bodart); 22,000 with Deroy's division of 10,000 (the adjutant's
    assurance of 18 December); the 2nd division 4,700 (Jérôme, 8 December); more than 30,000 with
    two units arriving (the Emperor, 7 January). The armaments Höpfner names make 25 pieces on
    10 December, 32 on 15 December and 16 for Vandamme's new batteries (our arithmetic), with no
    total.
  - The dates: Vandamme at Lissa on the 6th and the right bank invested on the 8th (Höpfner);
    Montbrun investing the left bank on the 7th and Vandamme arriving on the 8th (Foucart);
    Deroy's division about the 18th (Höpfner) or the 20th (Foucart); the capitulation signed on
    3 January at 9 in the evening (Foucart) or on 5 January after 6 (Höpfner); Bodart's end date,
    6 January, the evening the gates were handed over in Höpfner; the march-out on the 7th
    (Höpfner) or the garrison filing past Jérôme on the 8th (Foucart).
  - The talks of 25–26 December: an armistice refused, granted and ended by the governor because
    of the burghers' mood and side A's works (Höpfner); talks for surrender broken off on news of
    the Prince of Pless's reassembly (Foucart).
  - The storm: two accounts in Höpfner, side A seen at 6 crossing by cask bridges at the ravelin R.
    and the first raft in the ditch at 5 near the Ohlau gate.
  - The garrison's dead: 13 by Lindener's journal, beside the sortie's 1 officer and 10 men.
- **Gaps.** No targeted follow-up was run.
  - The most consequential gap is side A's strength and guns before Breslau. A return of the
    Silesian corps for late December or early January, or the French artillery's account of the
    siege, could close it.
  - Deferred:
    - Höpfner's Plan XVIII of Breslau, not inspected;
    - Höpfner's pp. 32–35 and 37–46 (the first move against Breslau and the governor-general of
      Silesia) and his account of the relief attempts (pp. 66–78, 82–85 and 88–108), not read
      beyond the OCR;
    - pp. 108, 113, 115 and 117, read in the OCR only, among them the day the frost set in, the
      governor's reasons 3–5, the heading and first items of the list of faults, and the break-out
      disposition Höpfner proposes;
    - not seen: the capitulation's text, the summonses, the intercepted letters, Lindener's
      journal, Blein's report, Du Casse and the *Geschichte der Belagerung von Breslau*.
- **Verification.** The pages and the draft were checked before release. The check:
  - transcribed Höpfner's and Foucart's pages whole from the images, every figure on
    full-resolution crops, and settled OCR misreads on the images, among them 'le 8' on t. II
    pp. 95 and 112, '12—15′' on Höpfner's p. 64 and '1806.' on t. II p. 97; it corrected the
    transcription notes before registration, among them the paragraph breaks on pp. 58 and 62 and
    a repeated phrase in p. 49's note;
  - reread every cited passage against its transcription and fixed what the reread found, among it:
    - Höpfner's 5000 real defenders are his estimate on stated assumptions, not a count;
    - the Emperor's order of 17 December went through the major general, and Jérôme was to send
      Deroy's division on Lowicz;
    - Vandamme had ordered his four new batteries before the bombardment of 25 December; the draft
      no longer says he built them, or that Duveyrier refused the armistice of 8 days on 3 January;
    - side A's firing only at the town belongs to the 20th, and the 'feint' at Neudorf was the
      gathering of the crossing material there;
    - Lieutenant Zimmermann had silenced the right-bank fire before the 11th;
    - the storm's two times are two accounts, not one timing dispute;
  - fixed the attribution of Höpfner's judgements: items 3–6 of the list of faults are given as
    the list's, since its heading on p. 115 was read only in the OCR, and his break-out reckoning
    now carries his own statement that it is a general point and not a charge;
  - cited transcribed passages that the first draft had left out, among them:
    - the governor's reply of 26 December to the burghers' petition;
    - the reports of the engineer of the place and of Lieutenant Schorlemmer on 30 December;
    - Vandamme at Lissa on 6 December and Blein's rafts;
    - the complaints of indiscipline behind Jérôme's arrangement;
    - the burghers' mood after 30 December and the artillery's conduct, with Blein's praise;
  - checked that no number in a value lacks a cited passage, which removed the storm's date from
    the strength claim and added the passages dating the summonses of 10 and 15 December to the
    defenders' command claim.

  The evidence check passes on all 2,991 sources and 46 drafts (916 claims, 15 unknown). The packet
  was regenerated, `make reproduce` was run, and `make check` passes (239 tests).
- **Usage.** About 1.9 million tokens by the session's token counter. The estimate for the
  cohort's other 279 engagements stays at about 165 to 190 million tokens.

## 1806 first pass, sub-batch 2e-i: Czarnowo (2026-10-18)

The first part of batch 2e of `fourth-coalition 1806`: the night combat at Czarnowo, where the
3rd corps crossed the Wkra at its mouth under Napoleon's direction and drove General Ostermann's
Russian force back on Nasielsk.

| Entry | Engagement | Date (Bodart) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B377c | Czarnowo (combat) | 23 December 1806 | 20 |

The main session researched, drafted and verified the entry without subagents. The work is
primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and the group's two families. Every cited passage was read from a registered
  page image ([source additions](source-additions.md)):
  - Höpfner, Bd. 3 (1851), newly registered, each page transcribed whole: Bennigsen's army by the
    report of 23 October, with the 2nd Division's composition and totals (pp. 23–24); and the
    Russian dispositions from 18 December, Kaminskoi's arrival, Napoleon's aims, Davout's
    preparations at the Wkra's mouth, Ostermann's position, the disposition Napoleon dictated, the
    night action, the losses and Höpfner's judgement (pp. 82–95). He gives the Russian losses as
    the Russians give them and the French losses as the French admit them, translates the
    disposition, and names no source for the position at Czarnowo or his estimate of 5000.
  - Foucart's *Campagne de Pologne*, t. I: the major general's letter to Davout of 22 December,
    half past eleven in the morning, Davout's letter without a dateline and the editor's narrative
    of the night of 22 December (pp. 372–376); the extract of the 3rd corps's journal of marches
    and operations on the night combat, with the order the Emperor dictated to Colonel Hervo and
    the table of losses of two divisions (pp. 390–401); and the major general's letter to Lannes of
    25 December (pp. 439–440).
  - Foucart's t. II: the editor's summary return of the troops in the December operations, on the
    unnumbered page of leaf n486, found by the one targeted follow-up.

  The pages were found by searching the three volumes' OCR. 73 records were registered: Höpfner
  Bd. 3's OCR parent and 36 page images (16 of Höpfner, 19 of t. I and 1 of t. II) with their
  figure transcriptions. Every registered page is cited. No figure in a value is taken from the
  OCR.
- **Not used.** Höpfner's second edition of Bd. 3 (1855) was compared in the OCR and is not
  registered. His p. 26, with the army's totals by the report of 23 October and his comment on
  them, was transcribed from the image and is kept for the Pultusk entry, unregistered.
  *Campagne de Pologne* t. I pp. 371, 388–389 and 438 were read on the images for the headings and
  datelines of letters that run onto the registered pages, and are not registered.
- **Draft.** 20 claims with 353 citations, all supported. Phases: 10 unresolved,
  4 commander_created, 4 post_outcome and 2 inherited.
  - The posture is coded `A_attacked` and tagged commander_created: Napoleon reconnoitred the Wkra
    and dictated the attack on the field. Side B's four attacks on Petit's detachment in the
    captured works and the last Russian attacks before the retreat, the journal's renewed charge
    and Höpfner's attack by Ostermann, were counterattacks. The attack Kaminskoi ordered for the
    24th was forestalled.
  - The fortification is coded `B_fortified` and left unresolved. Side B fought from batteries,
    breastworks, entrenchments, redoubts and abatis. By Höpfner the pioneers completed the
    position's works on the 21st, but the battery at the burnt bridge of Pomiechowo was already
    there, and no passage says who made the abatis. Side A's entrenched camp and bridgehead held
    only the reserve. A coding that counted Petit's defence of the captured works, or the
    bridgehead, would give `both_fortified`.
  - Both commands are commander_created. Side A's runs from the major general's conditional
    instruction of 22 December and Davout's preparations to Napoleon's dictated disposition and
    Davout's and Morand's orders in the action. Side B's is Ostermann's position and conduct,
    within Bennigsen's dispositions and Kaminskoi's orders.
  - The boundary leaves Nasielsk on the 24th, Augereau's fight at Kolozomb, the reserve cavalry on
    the upper Wkra, and Pultusk and Golymin outside the entry.
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - Side B's strength: 5,500 (Bodart); about 5000 for the position (Höpfner's estimate); 15,000
    with twenty pieces (the 3rd corps's journal); a division of about 15,000 on the Wkra (the major
    general, 25 December). The report of 23 October gives the whole 2nd Division 16,244 combatants
    before the campaign (our arithmetic).
  - Side B's guns at Czarnowo: 32 by Höpfner's list (our arithmetic); twenty in the journal.
  - Side A's strength: Bodart's 18,000 matches no passage read; the summary return gives the
    3rd corps 21,500 infantry and 750 cavalry in the middle of December.
  - Side A's losses: 850 with 16 officers (Bodart); 100 dead and 750 wounded with 58 officers, as
    the French admit (Höpfner, who assumes more); the journal's table for two divisions makes 807
    with 67 officers (our arithmetic).
  - Side B's losses: 1,400 with 3 generals and 38 officers, 500 prisoners and 5 cannon (Bodart);
    the Russians' own figures make 1,392 with 3 generals and 38 officers (Höpfner; our arithmetic),
    and about 500 wounded were the only prisoners (Höpfner), against a few hundred prisoners,
    500 wounded and 5 pieces (the journal).
  - The timing: the troops began to cross the bridge about 3 in the afternoon (Foucart's note); the
    signal after 4 and Morand's order to cross at 7 (Höpfner); the French fire opened at once by
    French reports and after an hour by Russian ones (Höpfner).
  - The date (23 December, Bodart; the night to 24 December, Höpfner), the river (the Bug, Bodart;
    the Narew, the journal and Höpfner), and the Wkra's bank in the letter of 25 December (the
    right bank, as printed, against the left bank in the journal and Davout's letter).
- **Gaps.** One targeted follow-up was run, for side A's strength. It found the summary return,
  which gives the 3rd corps but not the troops engaged.
  - The most consequential gap is still side A's engaged strength. A return of the 3rd corps for
    23 December, or Davout's report of the action, could close it. Side B's strength at the
    position rests on Höpfner's estimate, without a Russian return.
  - Deferred:
    - Höpfner's plan of the action at Czarnowo and his overview sheet, not inspected;
    - Höpfner's pp. 22, 81 and 96, seen only in the OCR, and p. 25, not read;
    - *Campagne de Pologne* t. I pp. 377–387 and 402–437, not read;
    - not seen: the 45th Bulletin, Davout's report, the Russian reports Höpfner draws on and the
      documents behind the summary return.
- **Verification.** The pages and the draft were checked before release. The check:
  - transcribed the pages whole from the images, the figures on full-resolution crops, and settled
    OCR misreads on the images, among them '25' toises on t. I p. 393 (OCR '26'), 'Zatowa' on
    p. 375 (OCR 'Zalowa') and the print's '(?)' after 'Kanals' on Höpfner's p. 88 (OCR '(2)');
  - reread every cited passage against its transcription and fixed what the reread found, among it:
    - Davout reinforced Petit three times; the great part of the 21st that then moved on Czarnowo
      is not said to have been sent to him;
    - the regiment whose cartridges were spent is the 17th by the sentence before, now cited;
    - the battery of 2 twelve-pounders faced the village of Pomiechowo, not Czarnowo;
    - the major general's letter of 25 December places the field marshal commanding the Russian
      army at Nasielsk without naming him;
    - Davout's account of the enemy at Ciexin is what he had told Morand;
    - Ostermann recognised the fire for what it meant; no passage says the French fires deceived
      him;
    - the attack on the Russian left and right is the journal's account of the plan, not the text
      of the dictated order;
    - Höpfner's version of the disposition gives the 17th's intervals as 60 paces, now cited;
  - checked that no number in a value lacks a cited passage, which removed a date from side A's
    command claim and a division number from the posture claim;
  - wrote out in each claim the renderings of citations grouped across claims.

  The evidence check passes on all 3,064 sources and 47 drafts (936 claims, 15 unknown). The packet
  was regenerated, `make reproduce` was run, and `make check` passes (239 tests).
- **Usage.** About 3.7 million tokens by the session's token counter, above any earlier batch's
  rate per entry: it registered a new volume and 36 pages. Recomputed for the cohort's other
  278 engagements, the estimate at the two finished groups' rates is about 160 to 185 million
  tokens. At the 1806 group's rate so far, about 1.2 million an entry, they would need about
  330 million.

## 1806 first pass, sub-batch 2e-ii: Pultusk (2026-10-18)

The second part of batch 2e of `fourth-coalition 1806`: the battle at Pultusk, where Lannes's 5th
corps, joined on its left during the afternoon by the 3rd division of Davout's corps, attacked
Bennigsen's Russian army in front of the Narew bridge.

| Entry | Engagement | Date (Bodart) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B377d | Pultusk (battle) | 26 December 1806 | 24 |

The main session researched, drafted and verified the entry without subagents. The work is
primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and the group's two families. Every cited passage was read from a registered
  page image ([source additions](source-additions.md)):
  - Höpfner, Bd. 3 (1851), each page transcribed whole (pp. 107–119): the march on the 25th, the
    Russian want of supply and ammunition, Bennigsen's decision to stand against Kaminskoi's order
    and Buxhöwden's return to Makow, the Russian force and order of battle, the ground, Lannes's
    reconnaissance and decision, the fighting on both wings, d'Aultanne's arrival, the night, the
    losses as each side gives them, and his judgement that the Russians won. He names no source
    for Bennigsen's motives, dispositions or strength.
  - Foucart's *Campagne de Pologne*, t. I: the end of a headquarters letter and the editor's
    narrative of the morning of the 26th (p. 462); Lannes's report to the Emperor of 27 December,
    with a footnote quoting the 5th dragoon division's journal (pp. 462–466); the editor's note that
    Lannes's report omits the 3rd division; the extract of the 3rd corps's journal of operations
    on that division's day (pp. 466–471); a loss line for the division; the editor's account of
    Davout's two orders, with Daultanne's pencil note (pp. 468–469); Daultanne's report to Davout
    (pp. 471–472); the journal's quoted entry for the morning of the 27th, the editor's comparison
    with Golymin and Davout's letter to the major general of 26 December (p. 473); and, as the one
    targeted follow-up, Lannes's letter of 28 December and General Corbineau's to the Emperor of
    the same day (pp. 514–517). The major general's letter to Lannes of 25 December (pp. 439–440)
    and the t. II summary return were reused from sub-batch 2e-i.

  The pages were found by searching the volumes' OCR. 58 records were registered: 29 page images
  (13 of Höpfner and 16 of t. I) with their figure transcriptions. Every registered page is cited.
  No figure in a value is taken from the OCR.
- **Not used.** Höpfner's p. 26, transcribed in sub-batch 2e-i, is not registered: p. 109 gives the
  Russian force at Pultusk. *Campagne de Pologne* t. I p. 461 was read on the image for the heading
  and dateline of the letter that ends on p. 462 (the major general to the grand-duc de Berg,
  Lopaczyn, 26 December, 9 in the morning). It is not registered, so the values call that letter
  a headquarters letter and name neither its writer nor its addressee.
- **Draft.** 24 claims with 428 citations: 22 supported and 2 disputed. Phases: 11 unresolved,
  6 post_outcome, 4 commander_created and 3 inherited.
  - The posture is coded `A_attacked` and tagged commander_created. Lannes decided the attack on the
    field, under Napoleon's order to take the Narew bridge. Side B's charges and counterattacks
    were made inside its own position: Koschin's, Barclay's, Baggowut's recrossing of the ravine
    and Somow's bayonet attack. Höpfner says the Russians nowhere made a counterstroke.
  - The fortification is coded `none_named` and left unresolved. The passages name no works.
    Lannes's 'artillerie formidable … à la tête du pont' places guns at the bridge without naming a
    work. The bridgehead in the order of 25 December was to be built after the crossing, and the
    one Corbineau names on the 28th was held after the battle.
  - The 3rd division joined on its own general's decision: Höpfner says without an order, and the
    journal says there was no time to concert with Lannes. It is not attributed to Lannes, and that
    claim is unresolved. Bennigsen's decision to stand against his superior's order is
    commander_created.
  - The boundary leaves Golymin (a separate entry), Czarnowo and Nasielsk before it, and the
    pursuit after the 27th outside the entry. The 14 pieces a Russian column abandoned near Moszyn
    on the 3rd division's march are reported with their place and time.
- **Disputes kept visible.**
  - The result. Bodart has a French victory, and the sources agree that the Russians left Pultusk in
    the night and morning after. But Höpfner holds the Russian victory hardly to be doubted, with
    Lannes repulsed at all points and back in his starting position. Daultanne reported the
    position taken but not held, and the 3rd corps's journal found the 5th corps back where it had
    started. Höpfner's account is kept as a disputed claim.
  - The Russian command. Bodart, Höpfner and Corbineau's hearsay give Bennigsen. Lannes reported
    the Russian general in chief commanding in person, with Bennigsen beside him. This is kept as a
    disputed claim.
  - Side B's strength:
    - 44,000 (Bodart);
    - 40,600 (Höpfner);
    - 40,000 to 50,000 infantry and 5,000 or 6,000 cavalry (Lannes);
    - 30,000 to 35,000 infantry and 4,500 to 5,000 cavalry (Corbineau, a general estimate).
  - Side B's squadrons and guns. Bodart gives 75 squadrons and 200 guns. Höpfner gives 55 squadrons
    and 7 foot and 2½ horse batteries; he could not determine the guns in action and rejects Prince
    Eugen's 150. Lannes reported about 50 pieces in battery.
  - Side A's strength. Bodart gives 24 battalions and 26,000. Höpfner gives 24 battalions and some
    20,000 for Lannes's corps, and about 30,000 once Gudin's 9 battalions arrived.
  - Side A's losses:
    - 3,300 killed and wounded and 700 prisoners (Bodart);
    - about 1,000, then more (Lannes);
    - 700 wounded and 400 killed (Corbineau);
    - 6—700 dead and 1500 wounded as the French give them, against the Russians' 7000 or, by the
      official reports, 10,000 (Höpfner);
    - 87 killed and 535 wounded for the 3rd division by its own line (our arithmetic).
  - Side B's losses and guns:
    - 3,500 and 12 cannon (Bodart);
    - 3500 by the Russian reports, against over 2000 dead, 1500 wounded and 12 guns by the French,
      and no guns lost in the battle itself (Höpfner);
    - over 3,000 left on the field and about 600 prisoners (Lannes);
    - 2,000 wounded and stragglers in the town (Corbineau);
    - 14 pieces abandoned near Moszyn and 23 pieces that Davout's order made Daultanne give up.
  - The Russian wings: on the town and the Mosino wood (Höpfner), or on two bridges (Lannes).
  - The dragoon journal's 'Le 22', as printed, for the day of the battle.
- **Gaps.** The most consequential gap is still side A's engaged strength. Bodart's 24 battalions
  and 26,000 match Lannes's corps without the 3rd division. No passage read gives that division's
  strength on the 26th beyond its 9 battalions, 170 horses and 2 guns. The one targeted
  follow-up, the letters of 28 December, added Lannes's revised losses and Corbineau's figures and
  hearsay, but no return. A 3rd corps return for late December, or the fold-out situations in t. II,
  could close the gap.
  - Deferred:
    - Höpfner's plan of the battle, not inspected;
    - his pp. 102–106 and 120–122, seen only in the OCR;
    - *Campagne de Pologne* t. I pp. 474–513, not read, left for Golymin;
    - the state of the Russian troops Lannes sent on the 28th and his position return, which the
      pages do not print;
    - not seen: the journals beyond the extracts, Daultanne's earlier report, Bennigsen's report,
      the Russian official reports and Prince Eugen's diary.
- **Verification.** The pages and the draft were checked before release. The check:
  - transcribed the pages whole from the images, the figures on full-resolution crops, and settled
    OCR misreads on the images, among them 'Garnowo' on p. 473 (OCR 'Gamowo') and the colon of
    'Tués :' on p. 471;
  - reread every cited passage against its transcription and the claims against the passages. It
    widened quotes so that each statement's subject is cited: Daultanne's opening sentence, with the
    division's efforts to second Lannes; Höpfner's sentences on the Russian cavalry in the gap,
    the broken battalions and the night fight he could not clear up; the village of Moszyn in the
    sentence on the 8-pounder; the first echelon's advance before the 14 pieces were abandoned; the
    hussar orderly's letter that turned Buxhöwden back; and the French guns on the height before
    their renewed attack;
  - cited the sentence that names the subject where Höpfner uses a pronoun or a title: Bennigsen
    before the right wing was taken back, Lannes for the reconnaissance and the order to take the
    bridge, and Baggowut as 'the Russian general' who retook his guns;
  - corrected three readings: Lannes's 'culbuté' refers to the column sent against his right, not
    to the Russian left; the ravine of Bialowizna is where the Russian line would have been thrown,
    not a feature placed behind it; and Buxhöwden's late letter to Bennigsen advised an immediate
    retreat, and did not announce his return to Makow;
  - checked that no number in a value lacks a cited passage, which removed page numbers from two
    values; kept Höpfner's '2¾ Meilen' unconverted; and wrote out in each claim the renderings of
    citations grouped across claims.

  The evidence check passes on all 3,122 sources and 48 drafts (960 claims, 15 unknown). The packet
  was regenerated, `make reproduce` was run, and `make check` passes (239 tests).
- **Usage.** About 1.2 million tokens by the session's token counter. The group has used about
  26 million tokens for 22 entries, about 1.2 million an entry.

## 1806 first pass, sub-batch 2e-iii: Golymin (2026-10-18)

The third and last part of batch 2e of `fourth-coalition 1806`: the engagement at Golymin. There
Augereau's 7th corps, the 1st and, from nightfall, 2nd divisions of Davout's 3rd corps and the
grand-duc de Berg's reserve cavalry attacked Prince Gallizin's Russian detachment. Gallizin had
halted before Golymin on his retreat, where troops of Dochtorow's 7th Division waited to take him
in, and Pahlen and Tschaplitz joined him in the afternoon. With it all 23 entries of the group are
drafted.

| Entry | Engagement | Date (Bodart) | Claims |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B378a | Golymin (engagement) | 26 December 1806 | 23 |

The main session researched, drafted and verified the entry without subagents. The work is
primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Sources.** Bodart and the group's two families. Every cited passage was read from a registered
  page image ([source additions](source-additions.md)):
  - Höpfner, Bd. 3 (1851), each page transcribed whole (pp. 101–102 and 122–126):
    - Gallizin's retreat on the 25th, Pahlen's retreat by Sonsk and Kaminskoi's orders to
      Buxhöwden's divisions;
    - then the Golymin section. His footnote names his sources (Gallizin's report in Plotho's
      Tagebuch, Danielewski, and the French reports in Mathieu Dumas) and calls his account only
      an attempt, because they differ so completely.
    - It continues with Gallizin's halt and dispositions, the French attacks, the fight for the
      woods, Pahlen's and Tschaplitz's arrival, the night retreat on Makow, and the losses by
      Gallizin's report.
  - Foucart's *Campagne de Pologne*, t. I:
    - the orders of the 26th through the major general (pp. 458–461, with p. 461 now registered);
    - Davout's letter of 26 December, with Captain Scitivaux's billet in a footnote (pp. 473–476);
    - the extract of the 3rd corps's journal of operations and two small-type lines after it
      (pp. 476–478);
    - Rapp's letter and the grand-duc de Berg's report, both of 27 December (pp. 478–483);
    - General Curély's recollections and the editor's commentary (pp. 484–487);
    - the editor's account of the 7th corps (pp. 487–490). By his introduction it follows General
      Desjardins's report and the 'Journal de marche du 3e corps d'armée', as printed.
    - the report on General Durosnel's brigade (pp. 490–491);
    - General Corbineau's letter of 10 in the evening and note of three quarters past 10
      (pp. 491–493);
    - as the one targeted follow-up, the grand-duc de Berg's letter of 28 December (p. 518).

    Pp. 462 and 473, Corbineau's letter of 28 December (pp. 515 and 517) and the t. II summary
    return were reused from sub-batches 2e-i and 2e-ii.

  The pages were found by searching the volumes' OCR. 64 records were registered: 32 page images
  (7 of Höpfner and 25 of t. I) with their figure transcriptions. Every registered page is cited.
  No figure in a value is taken from the OCR.
- **Not used:**
  - Höpfner's plan of the area of Golymin, not inspected;
  - his p. 100, seen only in the OCR, and pp. 103 and 127, not read. The sentences that run onto
    those pages are cited only in their parts on the registered pages.
  - *Campagne de Pologne* t. I pp. 494 and 519, not read;
  - General Lasalle's letter of 28 December, printed whole on p. 518, not used.
- **Draft.** 23 claims with 636 citations: 20 supported and 3 disputed. Phases: 12 unresolved,
  5 post_outcome, 4 commander_created and 2 inherited.
  - The posture is coded `A_attacked` and tagged commander_created. Side A's corps attacked a
    detachment that had halted before Golymin because its troops were too exhausted to march on.
    Where the passages name who ordered an attack, it was a marshal or general on the field. Side
    B's cavalry charges and its two battalions' attack in the woods were made in the fight for its
    own position.
  - The fortification is coded `none_named` and left unresolved. The passages name no works.
    Woods, bushes, a burnt village, a battery and an impracticable marsh are not works under the
    brief.
  - Side A's command is a disputed claim. Bodart names Davoust. The editor writes that the corps
    fought without any general direction during the whole of the engagement. A small-type line
    after the 3rd corps's journal says Davout received no order from the grand-duc de Berg during
    the whole affair, and the journal says darkness made any concert impossible.

    No passage read shows Davout commanding Augereau's corps or the cavalry. Each decision is
    attributed to the commander the passages name.
  - The boundary runs from Gallizin's arrival at 8 in the morning to the retreat on Makow in the
    night. Outside it are Pultusk (a separate entry), Czarnowo, Nasielsk and Sonsk before it, and
    the pursuit from the morning of the 27th. The guns and vehicles the Russians abandoned on the
    French march, and those taken on the road to Gotskowo, are reported with their place and time.
- **Disputes kept visible:**
  - The result. Bodart has a French victory, and the grand-duc de Berg reported the field yielded.
    Höpfner has no decision on either wing until dark. His account is kept as a disputed claim.
  - Whether Buxhœvden's corps fought at Golymin. It is kept as a disputed claim:
    - the grand-duc de Berg's letter of the 28th says the whole corps that had fought at Golymin
      had passed;
    - Corbineau's letter of the same day says that corps fought neither at Pultusk nor at Golymin;
    - Höpfner has troops of its 7th Division.
  - Dochtorow's force: two regiments by Danielewski, whom Höpfner follows, or the whole division by
    Gallizin's report.
  - Side B's strength: 22,000 (Bodart), against 50,000 by the general opinion the grand-duc de Berg
    reports. Höpfner gives no total.
  - Side A's losses:
    - about 1,000 with 4 generals and 53 officers (Bodart);
    - 644 for three divisions without the cavalry, from their loss lines (our arithmetic);
    - equal to the Russians' by the French estimate, and probably considerably more (Höpfner).
  - Side B's losses: 600 killed and wounded with 19 officers and 200 prisoners (Bodart), against
    3 officers and 80 men dead, 16 officers and 473 men wounded and 203 missing (Gallizin's report
    in Höpfner).
  - The guns, not added because the counts may overlap:
    - 13 pieces (Davout);
    - 26 cannon (the 3rd corps's journal);
    - 10 pieces and one more with a caisson (the editor's account of the 7th corps);
    - 4 pieces on the Gotskowo road (the report on Durosnel's brigade).
  - The times:
    - the attack ordered about 2 in the afternoon (Curély);
    - Augereau's 2nd brigade halted about 2 (the editor's note);
    - Morand's attack about half past 3, and Augereau's corps at grips an hour after nightfall (the
      3rd corps's journal).
  - Who repulsed the Russians at Sonsk on the 25th: the grand-duc de Berg (the editor), or
    Durosnel's and Wathier's light cavalry brigades (Höpfner).
  - Curély's Russian army from Pultusk, which the editor corrects to a column from Bielice.
  - The general killed: Fénérols (Bodart), Fénérolz (the grand-duc de Berg).
- **Gaps.** The most consequential gap is both sides' engaged strength.
  - Bodart gives 27,000 French and 22,000 Russians without battalions, squadrons or guns.
  - Höpfner gives no total and names the Russian regiments only in part.
  - The French documents give impressions, and the t. II summary return gives whole corps in the
    middle of December.

  The one targeted follow-up, the grand-duc de Berg's letter of 28 December, added his statement
  that the whole of Buxhœvden's corps had fought at Golymin. Corbineau's letter of the same day
  denies it, and in Höpfner only troops of the 7th Division were there (our reading). Gallizin's
  report in Plotho's Tagebuch, Danielewski's figures or a 7th corps return for late December could
  narrow the gap.
  - Not seen: the journals beyond the extracts, Desjardins's report, Gallizin's report, Danielewski
    and Mathieu Dumas.
  - Bodart's officer-loss table for the engagement is not in the frame transcription.
- **Verification.** The pages and the draft were checked before release. The check:
  - transcribed the pages whole from the images, the figures on full-resolution crops, and settled
    OCR misreads on the images:
    - Höpfner's '1½', '7½' and '2¼' (OCR '13', '74' and '21') and 'Kalęczyn' with its ogonek;
    - Foucart's 'SCITIVAUX' (OCR 'SOITIVAUX'), 'blessure' (OCR 'Llessure') and several '1re'.

    It kept printed forms: 'Lefrauc' beside 'Lefranc', 'Gostkowo' beside 'Gotskowo', and 'ordes'.
  - reread every cited passage against its transcription and the claims against the passages. It
    corrected:
    - Milhaud's and Klein's charges, which the grand-duc de Berg reports as charges, not as
      overthrowing the enemy;
    - Curély's 'coup de canon' and the order of the cry 'Halte!' and the brigade's break;
    - Corbineau's cannon that 'fired three shots';
    - several attributions: the editor's commentary on p. 487 against his account of the 7th
      corps; the two small-type lines after the journal; and the report on Durosnel's brigade,
      printed without a heading and signed by him;
  - labelled arithmetic and comparisons as ours, among them the return's 14,450 for the 7th corps,
    and Bodart's Russian 600, which fits Gallizin's 572 killed and wounded rounded up;
  - cited the sentence that names the subject where a pronoun or title stands:
    - Höpfner's 'der Fürst' and 'Er' for Gallizin;
    - Sarrut's brigade for the 'elle' that the Russian cavalry met;
    - the 7th corps account's 'maréchal' (Augereau) and the journal's 'M. le Maréchal' (Davout);
  - cited the sentence that makes Colonel Kreuz the rear guard;
  - checked each rationale's page list against the cited pages, which corrected several lists;
  - marked with their page the sentences that run across pages;
  - checked that no number in a value or open question lacks a cited passage, which rewrote one
    page reference;
  - wrote out in each claim the renderings of citations grouped across claims.

  The evidence check passes on all 3,186 sources and 49 drafts (983 claims, 15 unknown). The packet
  was regenerated, `make reproduce` was run, and `make check` passes (239 tests).
- **Usage.** About 2.1 million tokens by the session's token counter. The group has used about
  28 million tokens for 23 entries, about 1.2 million an entry.

## 1806 group check (2026-10-18)

After sub-batch 2e-iii the main session checked the 23 drafts of `fourth-coalition 1806` together.
The check was bounded and is primary-verified; no separate review was made.

- **Scope.** Scratch checks, run over every draft of the group and kept outside the repository:
  - every digit in a claim's value against the page-image quotes of the same claim;
  - every digit in a boundary note or open question, page references aside;
  - every English rendering marked as OCR that holds a digit or a number word;
  - each citation's locator against its section, and each draft's source families against the
    ceiling of Bodart and two more;
  - figures that one source gives for two entries;
  - how the drafts read Bodart's dagger (†).

  The passages behind each flag were read. Out of scope: re-reading the cited passages of each
  sub-batch, which its own verification did, and the leads that the sub-batches recorded.
- **Fixed: Bodart's dagger.** His explanatory pages (pp. 45–48) do not define the dagger. Some
  entries list daggered names under the heading 'Gefallene Generale' ('fallen generals'), as at
  Austerlitz and Auerstädt. Others print a daggered line with no heading, as at Saalfeld, Jena and
  Golymin.
  - Saalfeld (B371b) and Jena (B372a) read such a line as 'fallen' without saying that the reading
    was ours. Auerstedt (B373a) said 'fallen' without citing the heading its entry prints.
  - The same reading stood in three drafts of `third-coalition 1805`. Caldiero (B367a) and
    Dürnstein (B368c), whose entries have no heading, rendered the dagger as '+' and called the
    general killed. Austerlitz (B369c) did not cite its heading.

  The six drafts now cite the heading where the entry prints it and report the names as listed
  under it. Elsewhere they say that Bodart prints a dagger before the name and read it as fallen,
  labelled as our reading. That reading cites the heading of the group's entry that prints it:
  Austerlitz (p. 369) for 1805 and Auerstädt (p. 373) for 1806. The dagger is rendered as printed.
  Herbrechtingen (B364d) reports the dagger without reading it and is unchanged. Golymin (B378a)
  also leaves it unread, but its open question on disputes called Bodart's Fénérols 'the general
  killed'. It now gives the name as marked with his dagger, and the death from the Grand Duke's
  report. 13 claims and one open question changed in seven drafts, with 10 citations added: 4 in
  the 1805 group, which now holds 3,298 citations, and 6 in this one. No status, phase or code
  changed.
- **No change after reading.**
  - The first check finds no value digit in the group without a page-image quote in its claim.
  - The 77 boundary and open-question digits are:
    - 18 entry IDs;
    - 7 edition years and numbers in the notes on families;
    - 34 page numbers in lists the check does not skip, 29 of them in Jena's record of an OCR
      search, its hits and the pages read;
    - 18 figures, each a sum labelled as ours or a figure quoted in a claim of the same draft. The
      exception is Erfurt's '8 or 3', the hour of a dateline that the page image leaves uncertain,
      which its open question records as such.
  - The 65 flags of OCR renderings hold three kinds of text:
    - page numbers of neighbouring renderings;
    - text between renderings that the check's quote matching takes in across apostrophes, with
      page numbers and figures compared from other claims;
    - number words and ordinals such as the pronoun 'one', 'first', 'half', 'a dozen' and 'a
      hundred thousand men', which the figure rule allows.
  - Locators match their sections. Every draft cites Bodart and at most two other families.
  - Figures that one source gives for two entries are reported as covering both and added to
    neither. These are the 5th and later Bulletins' strengths and results for Jena and Auerstedt
    together, and the 29th Bulletin's 16,000 prisoners for Lübeck and Ratkau. The boundary notes
    of entries that share a day or a force place each captured body in one entry.
- **Recorded, not changed.**
  - The registry gives Foucart's *Campagne de Prusse* and *Campagne de Pologne* separate
    independence groups. The group counts them as one family, as sub-batch 2c-iv recorded, so Hameln,
    Nienburg and Groß-Glogau, which cite both, stay within Bodart and two families. Registry
    records are not edited.
  - Batches 2a to 2c-iii give some renderings inline or by reference ('as in claim …'), where
    later sub-batches write each claim's renderings out. A sample read in B374b found every quote
    rendered.
  - The group holds 23 drafts with 440 claims (426 supported, 5 disputed and 9 unknown) and 6,489
    citations.
- **Verification.** The evidence check passes on all 3,186 sources and 49 drafts (983 claims, 15
  unknown). The packets of both groups were regenerated. The 1805 packet's list of registered
  Napoleonic sources also gains the sources registered since it was last written. `make reproduce`
  was run, and `make check` passes (239 tests).
- **Usage.** About 0.4 million tokens. With the check the group used about 28.4 million tokens,
  about 1.2 million an entry.

## Methods checkpoint preregistration and workflow preparation (2026-10-18)

The owner accepted the project-assessment improvements and discontinued second-opinion prompts.
When asked about calibration, the owner kept the no-subagents policy and requested preparation
only. The exact messages are retained in `data/napoleonic/methods-v1/owner-decision.json`.
This work is primary-verified; there is no separate review.

- **Before execution.** `docs/napoleonic-methods-v1.md`, the frozen protocol and new experiment
  code are committed before computing synthetic recovery results. The seed, five scenarios,
  100 recovery repetitions and 20 validation repetitions per scenario are fixed. Tests use
  small constructed data only, including agreement with run 4's logistic solver. No historical
  Napoleonic outcome is fitted and no rating run or feature is authorized.
- **Campaign specification.** Three observations replay five original-language passages from
  already registered Alombert/Colin selections and three bound 1805 drafts. The primary read
  those passages in context. They establish local directives and overlapping legal/effective
  or superior/direct authority, not a complete campaign objective or replacement opportunity.
  Campaign-onset dates, eligible alternatives, inherited totals, information and the campaign
  outcome vector remain null. No historical contribution or readiness score is invented.
- **Schedule.** The outcome-unused audit keeps all 325 entries and 650 sides in the denominator.
  Conservative printed keys produce 375 labels, 75 with repeated appearances and 42 in more
  than one group. 197 entries have at least one cross-group recurrent label and 41 have both.
  Missing/joint/complex labels exclude 28 entries from the synthetic schedule, leaving 297.
  These are not resolved people or credited appointments. There is no verified cross-engagement
  army registry, so actual commander-versus-army identification is not assessed.
- **Evaluation.** A frame-only rule reserves the 52 entries of 1814–1815 in three complete
  groups; development is the 273 entries through 1813 in 13 groups. It preserves the frozen
  cohort and groups. Numerical model-selection reservation is not historical blinding. The
  protocol fixes broader-war and forward-time sensitivities and comparison against context,
  force, training prior and equal odds; a weak comparator cannot lower the success bar.
- **Workflow.** Brief v3 gives new drafts a checked side-by-side completion record, army
  continuity uncertainty, one follow-up disposition and an explicit stopping reason. The
  previous 49 drafts remain under their original contracts. Compact group packets retain
  printed entries and paths/hashes; per-entry and explicit full views retain access to every
  draft. The 23-entry 1806 packet is 224,233 bytes against its prior 5,217,415 bytes (about 95.7%
  smaller). This is measured navigation size, not evidence quality or measured token savings.
- **Calibration.** Frozen stratification prepares 47 claim tasks: eight each of 231 strength,
  166 responsibility, 107 coding and 464 other known claims, plus all 15 unknowns. Eight cited
  passages form a translation subsample. First-reading material omits claim values and author
  rationales/renderings; a separate public key means blinding is procedural. Judgments and
  reviewer remain null. There is no independent review or error-rate estimate.
- **Research screen.** The retained digit/locator/family checker emits 75 same-claim image-digit
  triage flags and one family-ceiling flag across the 49 legacy drafts; no quote or locator
  mismatch is found. Dates, clocks, ages, explicitly labelled arithmetic and French elliptical
  ranges explain many flags. The primary checked the flagged non-year quantities above 100:
  the sums are labelled as ours; Verona's 300, 1,200 and 600 render the cited elliptical forms
  `3 à 400`, `12 à 1,500` and `6 à 800`; the registered Koch page images 376 and 84 were also read. This is a bounded check of flagged interpretation,
  not a new verification of every legacy claim. The flags remain visible.
  - Dürnstein (B368c) cites four recorded family roots: Bodart, Alombert/Colin, Schönhals and
    Moriggl (the latter for Dupont's division). Registry notes already describe partial
    dependence between Alombert/Colin and Schönhals. The checker does not establish independent
    witnesses or settle that lineage. It retains this historical ceiling flag and every
    source/claim rather than deleting evidence to make the count pass. New brief-v3 passes
    exceeding three recorded family roots fail the completion check.
- **Preservation.** No existing source record, raw snapshot, dossier, frame, cohort, ledger or
  bound rating-engine module is changed. Calibrated/pilot-bound draft revisions must now archive
  their old bytes before correction, so these preparation records replay.

Before committing, `make check` passed 252 tests and the pipeline checks; `make reproduce` ran
offline. The final design also requires within-training-fold candidate selection and labels
reused development scores exploratory. The eight methods contract tests were rerun after this
clarification.

The next step within this checkpoint is the explicitly invoked synthetic job, followed by
inspection of all scenario results and the final offline checks. Research on 1807 is not part
of the checkpoint.

## Methods checkpoint execution and findings (2026-10-18)

The synthetic job completed under the design and code pushed in
`18262defc140e2796df677bb6639f73a7b06ea6d`, with no code, parameter or scenario changes after
results were seen. All 17 result bindings were checked byte-for-byte against that commit.
The execution receipt and saved log retain the actual command and result SHA-256.
The work is primary-verified; calibration remains preparation only.

- **Denominators.** 325 cohort entries, 650 side slots; 297 usable synthetic entries with 357
  conservative printed-label keys. The 28 excluded entries remain in cohort coverage. These
  provisional keys are not resolved commanders. Army identity is unestablished for every
  historical side slot; no commander-versus-army identification claim is made.
- **Execution.** Five scenarios, 100 recovery repetitions and 20 validation repetitions each.
  No historical Napoleonic outcomes, forces or losses were fitted. All synthetic results,
  repetition distributions, bindings, exclusions and environment are in
  `artifacts/napoleonic-methods-v1.json`; the Markdown report regenerates from it offline.
- **Predictive gain can be misattribution.** With zero true commander effects and persistent
  hypothetical army differences, the commander-minus-force held-out log-loss difference is
  -0.0136 by battle and -0.0135 by frozen group under war-year holdouts. Identical army and
  commander columns remain confounded even when both are supplied; priors allocate the effect.
- **Weak recovery.** With planted commander SD 0.6, mean centered RMSE is 0.5711, correlation
  0.2954 and slope 0.0884. Sparse observations, title/alias splitting, shrinkage and
  prior-dependent offsets limit this provisional experiment. This does not establish that
  historical commanders lacked differences or forecast a resolved ledger's accuracy.
- **Unhelpful cases retained.** Observing hypothetical switching army identities changes
  commander RMSE only from 0.5846 to 0.5811. The source-bias case worsens war-year held-out
  loss against force only by +0.0083; no favorable result is substituted. The null's
  corresponding difference is +0.0059.
- **Validation interpretation.** Whole-war validation holds out seven wars, but equal-group
  scoring retains the 16 frozen war/year units on its 297 scored entries. Forward-time
  scores cover 107 entries in five frozen groups. These are different test populations,
  and the repetition ranges are not historical confidence intervals.
- **Campaign and calibration.** The worked campaign remains not estimable, with null boundary,
  replacement pool, inherited resources/information and outcome vector. The 47 calibration
  tasks and eight translation passages remain unscored, with no reviewer. A later judgment
  record must be separately versioned rather than changing the blank generated sample.

Final verification passed: `make check` ran all 252 tests and the pipeline checks, and
`make reproduce` regenerated the result report and preparations without a model job. The
execution/log hashes and all preparation bindings replay. A checksum comparison of 4,793
original protected paths found only the intended CLI/packet code and build-receipt changes;
all existing historical inputs, earlier results and bound rating engines remain unchanged.

See `docs/research/napoleonic-methods-checkpoint-v1.md` for the bounded handoff. The checkpoint
ends here. The next research group is `fourth-coalition 1807`, with 19 entries and brief v3's
checklist. No historical rating fit, new predictor admission or deeper campaign investigation
is automatically authorized by these results.

## Roadmap record to 2026-10-06

Moved verbatim from `docs/roadmap.md` on 2026-10-06, when the roadmap was cut to the current
priority, open decisions and milestone status. Statements such as "Next priority", "the
immediate next step" and test counts describe the state when each paragraph was written; the
current priority is in the [roadmap](roadmap.md).

### Completed priority — full-war first passes (cohort v2)

Owner decision, 2026-09-25: after the first rating run found no detectable commander signal on
the 127-engagement pilot, research the rest of the war ("Sounds good, go ahead"). The
[cohort v2 frame](cohort-v2.md) covers all 384 source-listed engagements; the 257 new ones fall
in 83 complete campaigns. First passes follow the bounded protocol, by complete campaign, main
1864–65 armies first (Overland, Bermuda Hundred, Richmond-Petersburg, Appomattox, Atlanta,
Franklin-Nashville, Savannah, Carolinas, the Valley, Red River, Camden, Price, Mobile, Fort
Fisher), then the rest chronologically. Each campaign gets a separate Opus review. The v1 cohort,
baseline, ledgers and first rating run are unchanged; extending the ledgers and rerunning the
ratings on v2 need versioned successors and a new owner authorization.

**Status, 2026-09-25: first-pass coverage is complete.** All 257 new engagements have draft
dossiers (2,324 claims, 279 explicit unknowns, 11,976 citations, 660 disputed claims), each covered
by one of 19 campaign-batch Claude Opus 5.5 `high` reviews whose required findings were checked
and reconciled. The passes add 959 source records, 26 of them metadata-only review successors.
The versioned v2 successors of the strength and command-responsibility ledgers are also done
([addendum](ledgers-v2.md), [record](research/ledgers-v2.md)): 305 in-scope engagements (91 carried
from v1, 214 new; twelve records with a Native American belligerent are out of scope under the
two-sided rule), with new Livermore transcriptions under the owner's source decision, and sixteen
reconciled Opus reviews. Fit-eligible rows with both strength sides graded A–C rise from 37 to 126,
and 40 responsible commanders have two or more such rows (9 before). The owner authorized run 2 of
the ratings and the v2 estimate evaluation on the reviewed hashes; the [run
record](research/commander-ratings-v2.md) is separately reviewed. Commander identity lowered
held-out log loss under both weightings (0.6540 against 0.6633 battle-weighted, 0.6795 against
0.6844 campaign-weighted), so the report orders commanders; every interval includes zero, every
pair's 80% rank intervals overlap, and the result is not evidence of skill (design §5). On the owner's decision that battles should not be omitted, a reviewed
[grade E design](strength-imputation.md) models missing strengths from command level, side, period and
theater, and the all-battles test became the single verdict. [Run 3](research/commander-ratings-v3.md)
(301 battles, 162 with a modelled side, 20 imputations) again found lower held-out log loss with
commanders under both weightings (0.6476 against 0.6597; 0.6586 against 0.6651); only Forrest's 80%
interval excludes zero, and ranks remain widely overlapping.

**Follow-up, 2026-10-06: the last two unreviewed drafts.** On the owner's request, Antietam
(MD003) and Champion Hill (MS009), two of the three example drafts from the first commit, received
separate Claude Opus 5.5 `high` reviews ([Antietam](../artifacts/review-results/antietam-fb00e7d-opus-high-v1/primary-assessment.md),
[Champion Hill](../artifacts/review-results/champion-hill-fb00e7d-opus-high-v1/primary-assessment.md)).
The primary verified all eight required corrections against the already-inspected NPS/CWSAC
records. Antietam's three, with five advisories, are applied and its v1 draft is archived: MD003
now has 10 claims, 3 explicit unknowns and 18 citations. Champion Hill's five, with one advisory,
were first staged, because the frozen v1 and v2 strength and command ledgers bind MS009's v1
dossier by hash and cite two of the corrected claims, and the strength ledgers also bind their
checker code. The owner then accepted a reviewed [replay design](ledger-replay.md): frozen ledgers
replay in a temporary view that presents the archived bytes they bind. The correction was
installed the same day ([record](../artifacts/review-results/champion-hill-fb00e7d-opus-high-v1/installation.json)):
MS009 now has 7 claims, none unknown, and 20 citations, with its v1 draft archived. All four frozen
ledgers replay, and every authorized run (rating runs 1 to 3, both estimate evaluations and
grade E) reproduced its committed outputs byte for byte. The frozen ledgers still cite MS009's v1
claims; that is their record, not an error. Every dossier has now had a separate AI review. Both
records still rest on one source family (`nps-cwsac`); widening them would be a bounded
first-pass upgrade and has not been done. No model input changes.

### Previous priority — reviewed best-estimate strength ledger

Owner decision, 2026-09-25: first-pass coverage is complete, and the
[reported side-strength design](feature-admission-reported-strength.md) is accepted after
separate review. Its [extraction scoping](research/reported-strength-scoping-v1.md) finds
that the design would add very few rows (about 23–25 complete engagements, with two
current baseline rows dropping out as loss-derived). Owner decision, same day: "I feel like we should make our best estimate
for each." The [best-estimate side-strength design](strength-estimates.md) is accepted after a
separate design review and a focused follow-up: graded, source-traceable estimates with
ranges for each decisive, non-aggregate engagement, beside the unchanged admission
contract. The [estimate ledger](research/strength-estimates-ledger-v1.md) covers all
91 decisive, non-aggregate engagements and replays with `estimate-check`. Four campaign-batch
reviews and an engine review (Claude Opus 5.5 `high`, 2026-09-25) are reconciled: every
required finding was checked and applied. The reviewed ledger gives 21, 29 and 37
fit-eligible rows at grades A, A–B and A–C, against 23 frozen rows, and leaves 76 of 182
sides blank for lack of a usable candidate value. The owner authorized running the locked
evaluation (design §6) for that ledger hash on 2026-09-25; the authorization record applies
option (a), the only option available without an admission profile; the
[estimate-layer evaluation](research/estimate-evaluation-v1.md) is recorded as an exploratory
diagnostic. Strength alone is a weak predictor: worse than equal odds on the 21 grade-A rows,
and on the 37 A–C rows better than equal odds but not than the training prior
(campaign-weighted). The baseline is unchanged. Rating commanders next needs decision
responsibility per side, a campaign unit without double counting, and a residual model with
partial pooling (Milestone 3). The owner delegated those choices; the
[commander residual-rating design](commander-ratings.md) is accepted for implementation after a
design review and a focused follow-up. The
[command-responsibility ledger](research/command-responsibility-v1.md) names one commander per
side for all 91 engagements (A 155, B 8, C 16, D 3); four campaign-batch Opus reviews are
reconciled. The owner authorized the rating run for those hashes; the
[first run](research/commander-ratings-v1.md) found no detectable commander signal on held-out
campaigns, so no ordered ranking is given. More coverage is necessary but may not be
sufficient: a Civil War rating needs at least the other 257 source-listed engagements and more
usable strengths, and a larger run may still find no signal. See **Next priority** below. The coverage-first direction that follows still bounds any
further research depth.

Owner direction, 2026-09-20: the repeated Shiloh source investigations exceeded
the depth appropriate for a 127-engagement pilot. Park the Agate follow-up,
including the remaining article pages and original newspaper search. Preserve
all completed evidence, reviews and unresolved historical questions. The research
history below records completed work and deferred leads, not an active sequence
of further Shiloh tasks.
