All 36 frozen campaign groups now have first-pass dossiers. Every group's first
pass has a recorded separate review. First-pass coverage
does not admit features or change the baseline.

**Next priority (owner decision, 2026-09-25): a second-tier reported-strength profile.**
The strict opening profile cannot yield rows at feasible depth: at least 124 dossiers record
opening personnel as unknown, and Shiloh still has zero eligible candidates. The
[reported side-strength design](feature-admission-reported-strength.md) proposes
`reported_side_strength_v1`. It is a coarser, clearly labelled reported whole-side measure
that includes the baseline's CWSAC forces-engaged class, with its outcome-leakage weakness
stated.
It leaves the opening profile unchanged. A separate design review required nine
corrections and a focused follow-up four more; all are applied, and the design is accepted
for implementation. Next come a validator extension, typed quantities and one targeted strength follow-up across
all 91 decisive non-aggregate engagements. No rows are
admitted and no model inputs change until a reviewed release exists. Keep every engagement
regardless of outcome, reputation or available strengths.

Owner decision, same day, after the [extraction scoping](research/reported-strength-scoping-v1.md):
"I feel like we should make our best estimate for each." The immediate next step is the
[best-estimate design](strength-estimates.md), which sits beside this profile and changes
none of it; its separate review required ten corrections, now applied. The reviewed
[estimate ledger](research/strength-estimates-ledger-v1.md) followed; five reviews required
37 corrections, all applied on 2026-09-25. The order of the remaining tier-2 steps relative
to it is not recorded.

Use the [first-pass protocol](methodology.md#research-depth-and-coverage). Report
dossier coverage, dimensions still unknown, actual review scope and admitted rows
separately. Additional Shiloh detail is not the completion criterion.

### Milestone 1 narrative (as recorded in the roadmap)

The first [Shiloh source-enrichment pass](research/shiloh.md) is complete **as a
draft**, with 22 claims, 12 typed troop observations and seven chronology events.
Ten source artifacts from that pass include original reports, orders, return
transcriptions, two independently authored histories, and two facsimiles.
The subsequent [Confederate return audit](research/shiloh-confederate-returns.md)
inspected reports 136/137 and added four versioned source artifacts. The
[Union availability audit](research/shiloh-union-availability.md) adds 12 artifacts
and a unit-by-phase table. The [Ohio reinforcement audit](research/shiloh-ohio-reinforcements.md)
adds 17 artifacts and separates Savannah/opposite-bank arrival, crossing, landing,
formation and participation. The current draft has **62 claims, 40 troop
observations and 26 events**. Five preceding revisions are archived. Neither
canonical opening strengths nor effective command-transfer times have been
adjudicated. A separate fresh-context Astra `xhigh` source review is now recorded;
the dossier remains a draft. The four accepted corrections are implemented in
[schema v3 and versioned metadata](research/shiloh-review-corrections.md). Focused
review accepted the evidence changes; its sole validator finding was fixed and
accepted in the follow-up.

The versioned correction pass implements the four findings accepted in the
[primary assessment](../artifacts/review-results/TN003-a42f063-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md):
separate estimation provenance from printed precision, scope document dates to
individual report/diary/compiler sections, align dependence metadata for the same
OR p.112 return, and distinguish Crittenden's arrival from later debarkation.
The frozen evidence and review are preserved and the migration is documented.
The [focused review and follow-up](research/shiloh-review-corrections.md#validation-and-limits)
are complete under the [standing subagent policy](../AGENTS.md).
The [feature-admission design](feature-admission.md) and
[13 source-bound Shiloh cases](research/shiloh-admission-examples.md) now have a
separate Astra `xhigh` design review with no required corrections. See the
[actual review and primary assessment](../artifacts/review-results/feature-admission-838189f-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md).
The examples reference 26/62 claims, 27/40 quantities and 8/26 events and admit
zero features. An [offline validator and coverage ledger](admission-validator.md)
now implements the non-promoting checks. Astra `xhigh` implementation review and
[focused follow-up](../artifacts/review-results/admission-implementation-1264e21-followup-v1/primary-assessment.md)
are complete: three structural findings were fixed and accepted. All 82 tests
pass; no historical feature admission is implied.

The validator checks all 40 Shiloh troop observations: 18 blocked and 22 excluded,
with zero complete rows across the retained 127-engagement / 36-campaign frame.
Synthetic fixtures exercise positive reviewed-manifest replay and rejection cases.
The current baseline remains unchanged. A separate
[opening-boundary/population proposal](research/shiloh-opening-boundary-v1.md)
now revisits all 18 blocked mappings using 51 pinned passage anchors. Its explicit
v2 ledger has 7 blocked / 33 excluded; v1 remains unchanged. Eleven direct-use
exclusions follow timing/population evidence; zero complete rows are created.
Astra `xhigh` [accepted the bounded proposal](../artifacts/review-results/shiloh-opening-330599b-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md)
with one nonblocking precision clarification. The first-contact identification,
exact area and full memberships remain unresolved.

The [contact-and-location packet](research/shiloh-contact-location-v1.md) now
compares nine full participant reports (OCR context), 16 visually inspected
book pages and two maps cataloged to 1862. Its 14 attributed assertions and
two map-reading records preserve conflicting first-fire clocks, prior April 4
contact and map phase limits. There are 32 new source records; the draft dossier
and both admission proposals remain unchanged. Separate Astra `xhigh` review
found two literal errors; [versioned corrections and primary verification](research/shiloh-contact-location-corrections-v1.md)
close both findings without changing the historical assertions. There are now
87 source entries / 84 raw paths at that correction. The new
[April 3–5 contact-chain packet](research/shiloh-precontact-segmentation-v1.md)
adds 24 source records and recommends treating
April 3–4 as precursors based on return/reset evidence. Its explicit continuity
rule leaves the Saturday Howell link unresolved and qualifies weaker April 3
closure. Separate Astra `xhigh` review found two literal wording errors in one
Jordan passage, now [corrected in a new source version](research/shiloh-precontact-segmentation-corrections-v1.md)
and closed by primary verification. The registry at that correction was 112 entries / 109 raw paths.
The [Howell trace](research/shiloh-howell-trace-v1.md) adds Medkirk's retrospective
account, Worthington's disputed testimony selections, the earlier Reed printing,
one plaque transcription and two archival guides. Seven assertions bind 14
anchors; that packet's registry has 133 entries / 130 raw paths. No participant-to-Reed
attribution or Saturday-to-Sunday continuity is established, and no historical
feature is admitted. Original proceedings and commission correspondence remain
uninspected archival targets.
Separate Astra `xhigh` [review and primary assessment](../artifacts/review-results/shiloh-howell-67b5c30-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md)
accepted the bounded Howell packet with no required corrections; historical
provenance and continuity questions remain open.

The [regimental and post comparison](research/shiloh-regimental-posts-v1.md)
adds an overnight lead from Reid's 46th Ohio narrative but no located post or
continuous firing chain. The inspected 72d narratives skip Saturday; their omission
is not quiet. Worthington's distances remain disputed and approximate, and the
map cited by Medkirk depicts April 6–7. Seven assertions bind 21 anchors / 19
source-section pairs across 16 inspected pages. All prior records are preserved;
the prepared registry has 151 entries / 148 raw paths. Map control, full unit locations,
ford-guard identities and the transport/afloat rule remain unresolved, followed
by population/return evidence for the seven blockers. Continue across the complete
source campaign.
Separate Astra `xhigh` review covered all 7 assertions, 21 anchors and 16 images.
Two literal map findings are [corrected in a new source version](research/shiloh-regimental-posts-corrections-v1.md)
and closed by primary verification; the registry at that stage has 152 entries / 149
raw paths. Historical interpretations and all model/admission inputs are unchanged.

The [Reid provenance trace](research/shiloh-reid-provenance-v1.md) identifies the publisher collection process and Miller's general editorial role, but no original 46th Ohio witness. Lindsey supplies a later wording parallel with independence unestablished. Five assertions bind 11 anchors / 10 source-locator pairs, ten retained page images and one text-only LOC catalog snapshot. All earlier evidence is preserved; the registry at that stage has 164 entries / 161 raw paths and zero feature admission. Separate Astra `xhigh` [review and primary assessment](../artifacts/review-results/shiloh-reid-1c498e0-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md) accept all five bounded assertions with two nonblocking catalog clarifications: use the retained Agate letters label and provider page/line locators; material format and printed pagination remain unverified.

The [Agate comparison](research/shiloh-agate-comparison-v1.md) locates two historical reprints with 1864 title imprints, supporting a Reid/Agate account dated April 9, 1862. Its inspected opening reports Saturday skirmishing but does not supply the later B/K overnight narrative or identify its witness/post. The original Gazette issue remains uninspected. Five assertions bind 16 anchors / 12 source-section pairs across 11 inspected images; the registry at that packet has 176 entries / 173 raw paths, with zero feature admission. Separate Astra `xhigh` [review and primary assessment](../artifacts/review-results/shiloh-agate-73e26d4-astra-xhigh-v1/primary-assessment.md) accept all five bounded historical assertions; the sole documentation inventory finding is corrected and closed by primary verification. The original issue, overnight witness and continuity remain unresolved.

Historical admission still requires actual evidence-use review of a complete
proposal and an immutable release manifest; this partial proposal is no release.

The [actual AI review](../artifacts/review-results/TN003-a42f063-astra-xhigh-v1/review.md)
covers 62/62 claims, 40/40 quantities, 26/26 events and 26/26 supplied scan
selections. It found no substantive transcription mismatch in those selections;
35 of 65 cited source/section pairs remain text/CSV-only. This purposive sample
does not estimate an extraction-error rate or establish historical truth.
The [frozen handoff](research/shiloh-review-handoff.md) remains reproducible with
`make review-bundle`; actual review and dispatch records are stored separately.
The crossing clocks, handbook's 600-person basis, mixed-date reconstruction and
Reed's 7,553/7,552 discrepancy remain open. The earlier full omission mapping,
ten-man comparison residual, and Michigan narrative/table and casualty-day
conflicts also remain open.
The Confederate reports' differences from p.396, report 137's two inspected infantry
subtotal discrepancies, and the accounting of late/detached formations remain
explicit questions for historical review. The updated packet is
available with `python3 -m generalship packet TN003`. The bounded campaign pass
now adds Fort Henry, Fort Donelson and Corinth; follow the current priority above
for subsequent work.
Use Antietam to test outcome ambiguity and Champion Hill to test inherited orders
versus commander-created circumstances.


## Napoleonic strength/command ledger design v1 (2026-10-19)

Completed the owner's accepted bounded design using existing development-cohort dossiers.
The [scope](../data/napoleonic/ledger-design-v1/scope.json) selects the smallest complete
development group, then earliest year/key for ties: `third-coalition 1806`, four entries and
eight sides (Campo Tenese, Maida, Gaëta and Amantea). This is not a representative yield sample
or historical blinding. The Amantea interval remains December 1806 to February 1807.

- [Design](napoleonic-ledgers-v1.md): separate source observations, population/state mappings,
  command-role and identity mappings, discrepancies, transformations, verification and future
  release. Opening contact/area/availability are not supplied by frozen dates. No automatic
  rank-based commander selection, army continuity from names, source voting, imputation or
  historical fit is permitted by these examples.
- [Worked bundle](../data/napoleonic/ledger-design-v1/examples.json): all 31 strength/responsibility
  claims indexed, with 43 strength and 28 command-role observations. It is bounded excerpt work,
  not exhaustive atomic extraction. A frozen subset retains 53 complete existing source metadata
  entries and their parent/facsimile closure. All 46 distinct adopted passages were re-inspected
  on 26 pinned page images. No source record or raw file was added or changed.
- **Result:** 18 strength observations blocked, 25 excluded for the opening target; all 28 role
  observations blocked. All eight side-level opening mappings, army identities, responsible
  people and selected strengths remain unresolved/null. Zero complete candidate engagements;
  zero model rows emitted. These are applicability counts, not historical truth or quality grades.
- **Material distinctions:** Campo Tenese wider-corps counts versus local/compiler figures;
  Maida infantry/cavalry, rank-and-file and actual participation versus an all-arms opening target;
  Gaëta blockade/reinforcement phases, detached posts, one-sided bounds, prisoners and
  governor/acting-governor roles (Hotz/Storz correspondence unresolved); Amantea separately dated
  columns and town population versus garrison. Colletta's initial assault is month-only: the
  new mapping qualifies the old dossier's before-Bodart-dates shorthand and does not equate it
  to Koch's earlier attempt. The bound dossier remains unchanged.
- **Arithmetic:** 1,200+2,500+1,000=4,700 and 8,500+800+900=10,200 remain blocked proposals;
  4,500+300=4,800 is excluded as an incomplete all-arms population, with artillery people absent.
  No arithmetic result becomes a selected force.
- **Implementation:** a standard-library, offline, non-promoting checker and reproducible JSON/
  Markdown report; `napoleonic-ledger-design-check` is read-only and the full build/check includes
  its binding/coverage gates. Constructed positive tests are explicitly synthetic, with no invented
  historical approval. The 22 focused contract tests passed; final repository validation and
  preservation evidence are recorded in the [primary record](../data/napoleonic/ledger-design-v1/verification.json).

The work is primary-verified. No subagent, separate reviewer, second-opinion prompt, calibration
judgment, new synthetic job or Napoleonic historical outcome fit ran. All prior dossiers, raw
sources, frozen frame/cohort/evaluation partition and stored numerical results remain unchanged;
calibration stays 47 prepared/unscored tasks. The next recommended task is a bounded contact,
area, availability and ordering specification for the same four entries/eight sides, retaining
unknowns where existing passages do not establish a mapping, before expanding extraction.

## Naples opening-boundary/membership specification v1 (2026-10-19)

Completed the owner's accepted next step for the same four development entries/eight sides.
The [scope](../data/napoleonic/boundaries-v1/scope.json) binds the frozen frame, cohort,
evaluation partition, four dossiers and immutable ledger-design v1 records. No source was
fetched, added or re-registered; no dossier or prepared dossier packet changed.

- [Specification](napoleonic-boundaries-v1.md): one proposed contact/area/availability rule for
  both sides, with initial encounters, investments, formal battery openings and later states
  kept distinct. Named local areas have explicit outer-limit unknowns; no invented radius,
  march speed, synchronized clock or whole-town combatant population.
- [Source-bound records](../data/napoleonic/boundaries-v1/records.json): 40 adopted passages
  checked against 23 existing pinned page images, a frozen closure of 51 complete metadata
  records, 29 source-qualified events, 18 before/after relations, seven shared scenarios and
  30 membership cases. All 43 old strength alternatives remain indexed. These are worked cases,
  not exhaustive atomic extraction, independent review or a yield sample.
- **Result:** six conditional local component candidates, four area exclusions, one population
  exclusion, two timing exclusions and 17 unresolved cases. All eight complete joint opening
  mappings and force/person/army selections remain unresolved/null; zero complete candidate
  engagements or model rows. A component at an investment marker does not prove hostile contact.
- **Material findings:** Koch's approach contacts precede the main Campo Tenese attack; Damas's
  reported 2 pm is not a synchronized earlier-contact clock. Bunbury's Maida artillery exchange
  precedes the riverside skirmish and main clash; the 20th light company, Ross's body and tower
  guards have distinct membership questions. Gaëta's initial rifle fire, 4 March battery
  construction, July garrison fire and 7 July formal opening cannot be collapsed. Amantea's
  later departing columns fail an explicit 18 December investment scenario; the 4,700 sum
  remains unestablished for a later formal opening. Colletta's initial dawn assault is undated;
  only a subsequent night assault is explicitly placed in December. Neither is independently
  aligned to Koch's 3–8 December attempt or placed before 18 December. This refines v1's
  month-only shorthand while retaining the bound record.
- **Unknowns retained:** nonparticipation does not imply absence; prior presence does not prove
  continued availability; a later presence report does not establish an arrival clock. No edge
  is invented between Koch and Damas's clocks, Gaëta's July state epoch and the onset of garrison
  fire, or Reynier's column and an exact formal trench opening.
- **Implementation/verification:** an offline standard-library checker and deterministic

## Measurement feasibility checkpoint v1 (2026-10-20)

Completed the owner's accepted assessment of the two finished measurement groups:
`third-coalition 1806` and `peninsula 1812`, **12 entries/24 sides**. The
[memo](research/napoleonic-measurement-feasibility-v1.md) checks every entry's existing
population, boundary/membership and authority blockers and records exact input hashes.
This is primary verification of a records synthesis, not a new facsimile-inspection pass.

- **Finding:** no complete joint opening mapping is established in the existing packages.
  Their deliberately null selections and fixed zero-row outputs are not an empirical
  impossibility test. The two purposively selected groups cannot estimate corpus-wide yield,
  error rates or future research cost. The 273/52 development/final reservation is unchanged.
- **Reusable evidence:** Naples' four person co-references and the Peninsular group's six
  troop-total/role co-reports are different relationships and are not pooled into a quality
  measure. The latter cover six sides in six entries, with zero entries having indexed
  co-reports on both sides. Seven of 16 Peninsular sides, in seven of eight entries, explicitly
  retain unknown matched own-side local headcounts; Bodart's numbers remain visible. Badajoz
  still has mixed-population and infantry-only alternatives. Relaxing the clock alone would
  not resolve these population gaps.
- **Method distinction:** local observation retention, boundary-level responsibility,
  global identity, operational-entity continuity, admission and causal identification have
  different requirements. Exact clocks, complete tenure histories and inspected original
  returns are not universal prerequisites for every local measurement. An unopposed
  investment, a wider army, later participation or a diagnostic subtraction still cannot
  silently replace the strict target.
- **Recommendation:** pause wider strict-opening extraction and define the operational
  command unit and authority rules in one bounded specification, worked on six existing
  cases. Preserve unknown lineages and joint authority. Stop there before another extraction
  batch; a later source task must name the mapping it could change. The campaign-contribution
  aim still needs appointment, objectives, inherited-state and identification evidence even
  if battle opening counts become complete. A broader descriptive target is not adopted here.

or changed. No subagent, separate review, second-opinion prompt, calibration scoring, synthetic

roadmap now link the assessment and its recommendation.

## Operational command-unit and authority specification v1 (2026-10-20)

Completed the accepted [specification](napoleonic-command-units-v1.md) and six worked cases
from the two completed measurement groups. This is a primary-verified design over existing
assertions, not renewed historical extraction, page-image inspection or feature admission.
The parent denominator remains 12 entries/24 sides; six design cases are not a new cohort.

- **Unit decision:** a campaign comparison concerns a person's appointment over a defined
  operational authority/force scope and window. Force identity, appointment holder and
  locally available personnel are separate. Declare organizational level and distinguish
  containment, administrative membership, task attachment and identity; a campaign label,
  corps numeral, recurring commander or troop count cannot supply a default army ID.
- **Continuity rules:** retain evidenced organizational continuity through a change of
  holder while distinguishing the authority episodes. Reorganizations, renamings, splits,
  mergers and detachments need explicit evidence and qualified relations. Missing links
  remain unresolved; separate local handles do not prove distinct historical entities.
- **Authority/use rules:** local role retention, global person linkage, entity continuity,
  point responsibility, campaign appointment and numerical use have different requirements.
  Preserve source-relative timing, overlapping scopes and unknown effective transfers.
  Delegation supplies its stated task; a visit or support order supplies neither exclusive
  local command nor personnel availability. Tenure, analytical window and event time stay
  distinct. No invented clock, fractional credit or automatic whole-entry attribution.
- **Six cases:** Campo Tenese council/local direction; Maida expedition/field scope; Gaëta
  troop command and capitulation delegation; Amantea investment/later columns; Castalla
  reserve/sector/support tasks; July Salamanca subordinate task and Bonnet–Clausel succession.
  Stable parent record and passage IDs retain source IDs, locators and hashes. Each case
  states retained local evidence and the specific unresolved authority/entity/population use.
- **Result and limit:** zero new global person IDs, persistent army links, selected opening
  mappings, admitted features or model rows. The cases do not empirically validate a complete
  split/merger policy or identify commander effects. A later registry/release still needs its
  own implementation, primary verification and immutable migration records.

German-operations pilot's bounded appointment/mandate and prior-objective source check,

was started. Earlier evidence, source metadata, prepared packets and numerical results
remain unchanged; calibration stays prepared/unscored. No subagent, separate review,
second-opinion prompt or synthetic job ran.

## Rastatt mission window go-no-go decision v1, 2026-10-20

The owner accepted the bounded methodological decision recommended after the Murat
delegation check. The primary re-inspected existing passage `m010` and its complete
context `c07`, from `alombert-colin-t2-ocr-v1` in family `alombert-colin-1805`.
The [decision memo](research/napoleonic-rastatt-window-decision-v1.md) binds the exact
source/metadata, passage/context, parent record/scope and methods inputs. This is one
design case using one existing passage/context, with zero new passages, sources,
fetches or facsimile inspections. The parent 13-passage/ten-context record is unchanged.

**Decision: no-go for a historical replacement comparison on the current record;
close this candidate at the window gate.** The park-passage condition governs
headquarters residence at Rastatt. It does not supply an operative assignment start
or establish the full protection/observation/reporting interval. Guard authority,
instructions conveyed through Murat, personal observation near Kehl and the Emperor's
superior authority remain distinct. Park passage alone would not establish performance
of every duty or isolate Murat's contribution.

Four possible windows were assessed: document heading to passage for the whole
mission; arrival-to-passage for residence alone; operative assignment to the stated
residence-ending event; and calendar/planned-date alternatives. No window is selected.
Starting only at successful arrival would omit delay/failure to move headquarters
from an assignment comparison. Event-relative rules are permissible when properly
defined; they must address non-passage, changed orders and incomplete observation,
and must not fix the incumbent's completion time for every replacement. Earlier
preparation would be inherited for a later task boundary, not credited as total
appointment contribution. Uncollected resource/outcome values alone are not a failed
window gate. The unresolved operative anchor and duty/endpoint match drive this
bounded research-allocation decision.

The roadmap closes this branch while retaining positive task observations. No new
tenure search, alternative-window packet, resource/outcome extraction, admission or
numerical run is queued. Reopening requires a separately justified decision. This
does not establish corpus-wide infeasibility or alter the strict-opening target.
The 325-entry cohort, 273/13 development partition and 52/3 final reservation are
unchanged. Calibration remains prepared/unscored; no second-opinion prompt, separate
review or subagent ran. The decision is primary-verified.

Validation: exact source/context/quote and canonical metadata replay, the memo's
ten hash bindings, prior input bindings, local links/anchors, unchanged counts/nulls
and the receipt audit passed. **All 356 tests passed** (231.339 seconds), and full
`make check` exited successfully, including existing source and measurement checks.
No code, data input or executable contract changed, so `make reproduce` was not
needed; the offline reproducibility test passed and every prior generated artifact
retains its bytes. **All 8,014 protected prior tracked files remain byte-identical**,
including the receipt, original pilots, dossiers, packets, source registry, code,
tests and previous versioned records. The receipt remains at **1,153 inputs, 20

## Napoleonic compiler-strength benchmark run v1, 2026-10-20

The owner accepted the recommendation to authorize and execute the exact frozen
development-only proposal, replying **“Sounds good, go ahead.”** The new
`data/napoleonic/benchmark-v1/owner-run-authorization.json` records that reply,
the approved command/scope, proposal commit `88be0e7` and proposal SHA-256
`f33cbea825c477e6a831960cd43ff4da8d91391e1b8642b701c5f4faf970981d`.
The original preparation decision, proposal, protocol, dataset and executing code
retain their bytes. The explicit historical runner executed once, using CPython
3.14.7, and produced the immutable
[raw result](../artifacts/napoleonic-benchmark-run-v1.json), SHA-256
`35ed5cc7066cf40287e26afc03b16409a9012e81157b472abf212727faa5d505`.
It fits the 80 prescribed development folds across all four populations and three
evaluations. No parameter tuning, population selection or final-set evaluation ran.

The [results report](research/napoleonic-benchmark-run-v1.md) publishes every fixed
population/evaluation under both scoring weightings, the campaign bootstrap,
force calibration, campaign variation and signed error concentration. The raw
result retains all models' calibration bins, coefficients, predictions, folds,
group scores and engagement contributions.

**Grouped prediction improves, but temporal transfer is weak.** On the primary
194-pair population, campaign-held-out log loss is **0.559365** for force size,
**0.680716** for the training-only prior and **0.693147** for equal odds.
Equal-campaign log loss is 0.550669 versus 0.648723 and 0.693147. Primary Brier
scores are 0.190518 versus 0.243379 and 0.250000 by battle; 0.188712 versus
0.227805 and 0.250000 by campaign. War-held-out battle-weighted log loss is
0.560831 versus 0.683644 for the prior. These are predictions of Bodart's recorded
winners, not command effects.

The fixed 1813 forward view trains on 157 entries and scores 37. Its force log
loss **0.691122** nearly ties equal odds (0.693147), while Brier **0.251057** is
slightly worse than 0.250000. The training recorded A-win share is 107/157 and
the test share 13/37; the training-only Laplace prior is 0.679245 and transfers
poorly. The unqualified-total forward sensitivity trains on 133 and scores 30;
its force log loss 0.716775 and Brier 0.262276 are both worse than equal odds.
This is retained without changing eligibility or selecting a preferred variant.

The printed-indecisive exclusion removes no eligible rows and exactly duplicates
the primary result; it does not establish label independence. The non-combat and
unqualified-total populations retain 193 and 163 pairs. In the separate
163-entry campaign evaluation, the unqualified-total equal-campaign bootstrap
interval against the training prior includes zero: **[-0.127856, +0.013096]**.
The other planned intervals and both comparators remain in the report. All
intervals resample saved held-out campaign losses; they do not refit training
sets or model source/label uncertainty. No interval is claimed for the coarse
war or forward views, and no pass/fail skill rule was specified.

Force has lower log loss than the prior in ten campaigns and higher loss in three.
The top one/five/ten absolute engagement differences carry 2.32%/9.01%/15.73%
of the sum of absolute differences, retaining losses as well as gains rather than
dividing by a net improvement. Calibration reports the empty forward bin as
unknown and preserves the two-entry smallest primary campaign bin.

Primary verification:

- The new saved-result checker uses a second arithmetic implementation and no
  fitting. It verifies **80 fold memberships and optimizer score-equation pairs,
  1,629 saved predictions, 144 summary and 480 group metric values, 360 calibration
  bins and all 32 bootstrap endpoints**, including training-only priors and
  reserved-final separation. Its
  [verification record](../artifacts/napoleonic-benchmark-run-v1-verification.json)
  binds the result, authorization, proposal and checker code. This is primary
  verification, with no separate review.
- All **four new focused tests** passed: altered predictions/metrics, contaminated
  training membership and changed bootstrap bounds are rejected; the valid saved
  run reconciles with the optimizer mocked and uncalled. These checks perform no
  historical fit. The ordinary benchmark unit fixtures remain constructed cases.
- All **384 tests passed** (259.438 seconds). Full `make check` and `make reproduce`
  exited successfully. Both commands now verify the saved run; reproduction
  regenerates only its readable report/verification record, never the historical
  result. Every report file link resolves and the generated report was inspected.
- The pre-run audit covered **8,033 tracked files**. Only Makefile, README, roadmap,
  this log and the receipt change among those files. All prior raw evidence,
  metadata, dossiers, packets, versioned records and numerical reports retain
  their bytes. All **20 prior receipt output hashes**, **5,667 source bindings**
  and every prior input hash are unchanged. The receipt has **1,165 inputs**,

  1,163. The new run and verification retain their separate bindings.

All **52 final-evaluation entries remain reserved and unused numerically**.
Source dependence, selection, mixed populations, rounding and strength-dependent

## Napoleonic 1813 forward diagnostic v1, 2026-10-20

The owner accepted the bounded next recommendation, replying **“Sounds good, go
ahead.”** The new [scope](../data/napoleonic/forward-diagnostic-v1/scope.json)
binds the completed benchmark run and inputs. This is exploratory diagnosis of
saved predictions, not another model run or pre-registered confirmation. The
[memo](research/napoleonic-forward-diagnostic-v1.md) and
[machine-readable diagnostic](../artifacts/napoleonic-forward-diagnostic-v1.json)
retain all **64 development entries in 1813**, the **37 saved primary forecasts**
and **27 excluded entries**, including their reasons and recorded outcomes.
No historical refit, recalibration or new forecast is produced. All **52 final
entries remain reserved and unused numerically**.

Coverage falls from **157/209 (75.12%)** before 1813 to **37/64 (57.81%)** in
1813. The complete development frames' recorded French/allied win shares are
144/209 before 1813 and 18/64 in 1813; the eligible shares are 107/157 and 13/37.
Thus the shift persists outside the eligible sample; eligibility raises the
1813 share. The 1813 frame has 128 side records: 89 eligible side totals, 27
missing extracts and 12 ambiguous extracts. Fifteen otherwise eligible sides
lack an eligible opponent. Excluded entries are not silently discarded or
assigned zero forces. Liberation contributes 29/52 eligible entries and
Peninsula 8/12, leaving 23 and four excluded respectively.

**Ordering and average calibration differ.** The saved force probabilities
average **0.623880**, against recorded A-win share **0.351351**, an average
overprediction of **27.25 percentage points**. Pair-count AUC is **247/312 =
0.791667**, with no tied test pairs. Liberation's AUC is 0.826316 on 29 cases,
Peninsula's 0.600000 on eight. Pooled pairs share entries and include
cross-campaign comparisons; no confidence or uniform-transfer claim follows.
The training force-balance association is descriptive, not a new validation.
French/allied forces are smaller in 26/37 test entries, versus 77/157 earlier;
their recorded win share within that broad stratum falls from 38/77 to 4/26.
The strata do not hold exact force balance or historical context fixed and
identify no historical mechanism.

The exact Brier variance/covariance identity reconciles the saved **0.251056881**
with equal odds **0.250000000**. Relative squared mean bias contributes
**+0.052175701** and prediction variance minus twice covariance contributes
**−0.051118820**, totaling **+0.001056881**. This is arithmetic accounting,
not a causal decomposition, a fitted calibration curve or proof that changing
the intercept would generalize. The original five calibration bins remain
unchanged, including the empty bin as unknown and the seven-case highest bin.
Original log loss remains 0.691121738 versus equal odds 0.693147181.

The paired sensitivity comparison uses the two **already-saved** forward
models. Primary log loss is 0.713348147 on the common 30 entries and 0.595865700
on the removed seven, reconciling to 0.691121738 over all 37. The saved
unqualified-total model scores 0.716775370 on the common 30. Its worsening of
0.025653632 relative to the primary-all result splits into **+0.022226409 from
case removal** and **+0.003427223 between saved models on the same rows**.
Brier gives the same accounting. Different training populations remain a
limitation; these are not causal effects of source annotations or a preferred
variant selection.

The five largest primary forward log losses already cover both 1813 campaigns:
**B447b Möckern, B457a Vera, B459d Wartenburg, B455b Hagelberg and B448a
Castalla**. The primary re-read their complete existing transcription entries
and visually compared all five pinned pages (447, 457, 459, 455 and 448),
including applicable notes and neighboring-entry boundaries. Their literal
totals, side mapping and recorded outcomes match. No mechanical correction
was found in this purposive five-case sample; this neither verifies the other
32 test entries nor adjudicates historical truth. The new
[inspection record](../data/napoleonic/forward-diagnostic-v1/evidence-checks.json)
retains exact passages, English renderings, source IDs/locators, raw and
metadata SHA-256s, image hashes and note-applicability findings. Text source
`bodart-1908-transcription-v1` retains raw SHA-256
`bf12437b6d19226ce127ce5d81e59d20358a5b1999ffe00f5ac6a8f8c20ef986`.
No source was fetched, registered or revised; no source-additions entry or
prepared-packet update is needed.

Primary arithmetic checks also used a separate rank-sum AUC calculation and
exact rational arithmetic for both Brier identities. They agree with the new
pair-count and floating-point implementations. Eight focused tests cover ties
and undefined single-class AUC, Brier accounting, paired-score decomposition,
campaign-aware error selection, frozen-input tampering, altered passage
figures/quotations/image hashes, full saved membership and final separation.
The optimizer is mocked and verified uncalled in the saved-diagnostic test.
Derived floating-point comparisons allow absolute rounding error up to `2e-12`
across Python/platforms; structure, types, counts and SHA-256 bindings remain
exact. A constructed comparison test rejects larger numeric changes and
changed bindings or count types.

The preservation audit covers **8,039 prior tracked files**. Only Makefile,
README, roadmap, this log and the receipt change among them. All **1,165 prior
receipt input hashes**, **5,667 source bindings** and **20 output hashes** are
unchanged, including the original dataset, run, proposal, authorization and
verification records. The receipt now has **1,168 inputs**, adding only the
new scope, actual inspection record and diagnostic module. The diagnostic
binds all 14 of its inputs, including both verifier modules; its scope records
the post-implementation freeze explicitly. All 37 saved forecasts are also
compared directly with the original result and remain exactly identical.
Generated memo links resolve and its coverage, scores, arithmetic tables and
passages were inspected by the primary.

All **392 tests passed** (249.950 seconds), including all eight new focused
tests. Full `make check` and `make reproduce` exited successfully after the
final scope/code freeze. The saved diagnostic path verifies the original
80-fold/1,629-prediction run without fitting and regenerates only its own
arithmetic JSON and memo. All prior versioned records and generated reports
retain their bytes.

**Recommendation:** design one development-only rolling-forward calibration
comparison with training-only correction, fixed folds/support, comparators,
coverage and a decision rule before any further fit. The already-inspected
1813 result is exploratory. This task authorizes neither that future run nor
use of the final reservation. Compiler selection, strength-dependent winner
coding, one source family, uncertain populations and missing pairs remain
limits. No feature is admitted, no commander rating or causal effect is
estimated, wider strict-opening work remains paused and the appointment branch
stays closed. Extraction-review calibration remains prepared and unscored;
no subagent, separate review or second-opinion prompt ran.

## Napoleonic temporal probability-correction design v1, 2026-10-20

The owner accepted the recommendation to design one fixed, training-only
probability-correction test, replying **“Sounds good, go ahead.”** The new
[scope](../data/napoleonic/temporal-calibration-v1/scope.json) records that actual
preparation request and explicitly withholds authorization for historical fitting.
The [design](napoleonic-temporal-calibration-v1.md) and
[prepared plan](../artifacts/napoleonic-temporal-calibration-plan-v1.md) are
primary-verified. No historical outcome was loaded into a fit, no historical
forecast was generated, and no run-authorization record or historical result
was created.

The date audit uses all **273 development entries / 13 frozen source groups**,
including numerically excluded members. Several groups extend beyond their
source-year label: the 1810 Peninsular group ends in 1812, for example. A group
becomes available only after every member has a known end strictly before
January 1 of the target year. The latest nonempty completion-year block is
calibration; earlier complete groups are the base. The base must also finish
before every calibration member begins. Unknown dates, overlap or fewer than
ten eligible rows in either training block make a fold not estimable, without
another window or a member-level repair. The original groups are never split
or reassigned. These are retrospective event-date safeguards, not proof of
publication dates or contemporary information availability.

The single candidate fits the unchanged ridge-1 force model on base rows,
freezes both coefficients, then estimates one ridge-1 additive logit offset on
the disjoint calibration block. The base stays frozen afterward. A strictly
convex one-parameter objective and score-equation check define the correction;
it cannot improve the base's ordering within a fold. Comparators are the same
uncorrected base, expanding force, expanding and recent Laplace priors, and
equal odds. This prevents success from being defined only against an older,
smaller force fit or a weak base-rate comparator.

The main decision region is **1808–1812: 115/141 primary entries**, five annual
folds and seven complete source groups. All 26 exclusions remain. The 68-entry
1805–1807 warmup has 42 primary-eligible entries and no outer score. The reused
1813 region retains **37/64 entries** and 27 exclusions as secondary diagnostics
only. Its scores cannot enter or rescue the continuation decision. Two eligible
development operations end in 1814; their IDs remain development under the
unchanged start-cohort reservation. All **52 final entries remain unused
numerically**. The unqualified-total region has 103/141 main and 30/64 secondary
entries. All four existing populations are retained, including the indecisive
no-op and the non-combat exclusion.

The fixed exploratory rule requires lower main log loss and nonhigher Brier
against every comparator under battle and equal-campaign weighting, improvement
against expanding force in at least three of five years, and no worsening
against expanding force in the unqualified-total sensitivity under either score
or weighting. Missing required results mean not assessable. The rule is not a
significance test, feature-admission contract or commander-rating gate. The
small number of folds/groups, repeated calibration blocks, overlapping training,
one compiler family and prior exposure of development outcomes remain explicit.
No bootstrap confidence claim or best-window selection is proposed.

The exact [run proposal](../data/napoleonic/temporal-calibration-v1/run-proposal.json)
has SHA-256 **`b6b58138ba1b396160a0d9185e18a3b756f83788fb89da5f3431000a9d84b5f7`**
and binds **16 files**, including the design, protocol, source dataset, executing
code and both plan outputs. A future owner-authorized run proposes **72 parameter
fits over 24 scorable population/year folds**. The runner checks the exact later
authorization before reading outcome labels or invoking either fitter and refuses
to overwrite the new result path. Checks/reproduction use only the planner;
tests fit constructed cases only. A separate direct metadata calculation
reconciled all **36 population/year splits** with the plan.

All **13 focused tests passed**. They cover complete-group deferral caused by an
ineligible late/unknown member, strict cutoff and base/calibration separation,
duplicate/split groups, all denominators/final separation, input/proposal tampering,
exact restoration of missing/corrupt outputs, offset score equations/objective and
complement behavior, single-class/invalid inputs, held-out label and provenance
poisoning, training-only priors, continuation failures/tolerances/sensitivity veto,
exclusion of 1813 from the decision and rejection of absent/mismatched approval
before labels or fitting. Positive authorization parsing uses a constructed
temporary root with no historical inputs, never an owner approval record.

All **405 tests passed** (252.140 seconds), and full `make check` and
`make reproduce` exited successfully. The final 13 focused tests also passed
after making their protected-file assertion preserve any later legitimate
authorization/result rather than assume those files can never exist. The
prepared outputs were inspected, their links resolve, and exact restoration
reproduces both pinned hashes. The proposal remains unchanged.

The preservation audit covers **8,045 prior tracked files**. Only Makefile,
roadmap, this log and the receipt change among them; README remains unchanged.
All **1,168 prior receipt input hashes**, **5,667 source bindings** and **20
output hashes** retain their bytes. The receipt now has **1,173 inputs**, adding
only the three new preparation JSON files and two Python modules. The design
and two plan outputs have their separate proposal bindings. All prior source,
dossier, packet, protocol, authorization, numerical result and 1813 diagnostic
files are unchanged. Preparation ends at the exact executable proposal;
the next action is its separately authorized run.

Date provenance remains the unchanged `bodart-1908-transcription-v1`, raw SHA-256
`bf12437b6d19226ce127ce5d81e59d20358a5b1999ffe00f5ac6a8f8c20ef986`, and frozen
frame SHA-256 `af6016fc4d8505de0e5ebba4d3cd106178a07dd542c4c6ab7ed34770f1af75ea`.
The plan retains controlling entry/field locators and hashes. This task checks
encoded metadata and its temporal use; no new page inspection, date adjudication,
source fetch, registry change or prepared evidence-packet update occurs. No
source-additions entry is needed. All earlier evidence, model inputs, authorizations,
numerical results and the 1813 diagnostic remain unchanged. Wider opening work,
the closed appointment branch and unscored extraction-review calibration stay
parked. No second-opinion prompt, separate review or subagent ran.

year/group scores, paired losses, concentration and the fixed decision. The
[report](research/napoleonic-temporal-calibration-run-v1.md) is primary-verified;
there was no separate review.

**The correction fails the fixed exploratory continuation rule.** Main
1808–1812 coverage remains **115/141 entries**, five years/seven source groups,
with 26 exclusions. Battle-weighted corrected log loss is **0.663652398** versus
**0.632990740** for expanding force; Brier is **0.234834355** versus
**0.223525340**. Equal-campaign scores worsen too: log loss **0.638446083**
versus **0.623784780**, Brier **0.224998624** versus **0.220290107**. Against
the same uncorrected base, battle scores worsen; the tiny equal-campaign
log-loss improvement is accompanied by worse Brier, so that comparison also
fails. Improvements against the prior comparators and equal odds are retained,
but do not satisfy a rule requiring success against every comparator.

Only **1810 and 1811 improve against expanding force (2/5 years, required ≥3)**.
The unqualified-total sensitivity has **103/141 main entries** and worsens
against expanding force under both scores/weightings: battle log loss
**0.684243174** versus **0.653678666**, Brier **0.242120839** versus
**0.232353626**. The printed-indecisive exclusion remains a no-op; removing
one non-combat warmup row changes subsequent fits without rescuing the result.
All six failed aggregate comparisons and all six satisfied comparisons remain
in the decision record. No population or model is selected after execution.

The reused secondary **1813 region remains 37/64 entries**. Its primary
corrected log loss improves to **0.601048725** from **0.691121738** for expanding
force, and Brier to **0.207721280** from **0.251056881**. These favorable scores
cannot enter or rescue the continuation decision. The unqualified secondary
has **30/64 entries**. The 68-entry warmup has no outer score, including its
42 primary-eligible entries. Every region/exclusion remains visible. All
**52 final entries stay numerically unused**, including no calibration,
selection or scoring. The two development operations ending in 1814 keep
their frozen start-cohort IDs; no reserved entry is reclassified.

The 24 scorable population/year folds performed **72 parameter fits**:
48 force fits and 24 offsets. Deliberate repeated blocks and the indecisive
no-op remain repeated fit calls, not independent replications. There are
**589 population-entry prediction rows / 3,534 probability values**; entries
repeat across sensitivities. Within each fold the offset preserves base
ordering. The primary correction improves log loss in three of seven main
source groups and worsens in four. Against expanding force, the largest one,
five and ten absolute main paired differences carry **3.53%, 13.54%, 24.86%**
of the **15.383860** absolute-difference total. The denominator retains gains
and losses without cancellation and assigns no command credit.

The new [saved-result verifier](../generalship/napoleonic_temporal_calibration_report.py)
performs no fitting. A second arithmetic implementation checks all **36 annual
role assignments**, using every member for group completion and earliest
calibration start; all **48 force and 24 offset score equations**; every
prediction; **80 summaries / 1,632 score values / 4,800 calibration bins**;
**2,945 paired case contributions**, every concentration record and the fixed
continuation decision. Maximum absolute force score-equation residual is
`3.277378368693462e-13`; offset residual is `8.49364356625415e-10`, within the
fixed tolerances of 1e-7 and 1e-9. Saved arithmetic tolerance is 2e-12.
These observed residuals are from the CPython 3.14.7 primary check; the durable
verification record retains checked counts/tolerances rather than residual
last bits that can differ across Python versions and math libraries.
The verifier matches **141 original 1813 prediction rows** across all four
populations without refitting. The new equal-campaign secondary summary
weights the two original source groups, while the old forward report used
one held-out year; that weight difference is explicit.

All **21 focused tests passed**, including eight new saved-result tests.
They reject changed time roles/training contamination, force/offset parameters,
probabilities, both weighted scores, empty-bin zero substitution, paired losses,
concentration and a relabelled decision. They verify that neither fitter nor
the historical runner's outcome loader is called during saved verification,
and that arbitrary secondary scores cannot change the separate decision
recomputation. Historical refits during verification remain zero. Tests use
constructed fits only; the new tests inspect saved results without fitting.

The six existing convention passages were rechecked against the unchanged
transcription and three pinned images, printed pp. 45–47. Source ID remains
`bodart-1908-transcription-v1`, raw SHA-256
`bf12437b6d19226ce127ce5d81e59d20358a5b1999ffe00f5ac6a8f8c20ef986`;
the existing context retains exact locators, quotes and image hashes. The
scope/measurement/label limits remain: compiler selection, strength-dependent
winner coding, rounded or qualified totals, missing pairs, uncertain force
clock/population, retrospective date availability and familiar development
outcomes. Five main years/seven groups, repeated blocks, overlapping training
and one compiler family limit replication. No significance or confidence claim
is made. No new source, date adjudication, registry change or dossier/packet
evidence change occurs, so no source-additions entry or packet update is needed.

**Disposition:** retain the uncorrected descriptive baseline and close this
correction branch. Failure does not trigger another correction/window search,
final-set evaluation, commander rating or wider historical extraction. No
feature is admitted or tactical/campaign effect estimated. All earlier evidence,
numerical records and final reservations are preserved; the stopped appointment
branch, paused opening work and prepared/unscored extraction-review sample remain
unchanged. No second-opinion prompt, separate review or subagent ran.

All **413 tests passed** (267.576 seconds), and final full `make check` and
`make reproduce` exited successfully. The final 21 focused tests passed after
the verification-record portability fix. Reproduction uses only the frozen
metadata planner and saved-result verifier for this job; it does not fit the
historical correction again. The generated report's scores, coverage, decision,
limits and eight relative links were inspected.

The preservation audit covers **8,055 prior tracked files**. Only Makefile,
README, roadmap, this log and the receipt change among them; six new files

and tests. All **1,173 prior receipt input hashes**, **5,667 source bindings**

## 1805 loss-measurement feasibility pilot v1, 2026-10-20

The owner accepted the bounded recommendation to audit all **22 entries of
`third-coalition 1805`** using existing pinned passages, preserve loss categories,
missingness and disputes, and end with one go/no-go decision on a comparable
loss-based outcome. The verbatim reply was **“Sounds good, go ahead.”** The
new [scope](../data/napoleonic/loss-pilot-v1/scope.json) records that actual
authorization and the exact existing development IDs. The scope and
[protocol](../data/napoleonic/loss-pilot-v1/protocol.json) were written before
the complete loss extraction. No historical fit, final-set numerical use,
new source fetch or broader extraction is authorized. One controlling source
family, `bodart-kriegs-lexikon-1908`, is used for numerical extraction.

The primary inspected all **eight entry images, printed pp. 363–370**, and
**three convention images, pp. 45–47**. The new
[source record](../data/napoleonic/loss-pilot-v1/observations.json) retains
**72 entry/footnote passages**, **seven convention passages**, exact locators,
source metadata hashes, image IDs/hashes and **140 typed quantities**. This is
primary verification, not a separate historical review. Copies and page images
remain one family. All 22 entries / 44 A/B sides are retained, including the
nine sides with no numerical personnel-loss observation.

**Decision: no-go for one common loss-based grade across the complete group.**
The [report](research/napoleonic-1805-loss-feasibility-v1.md) and
[measurement JSON](../artifacts/napoleonic-1805-loss-pilot-v1.json) distinguish
source presence from comparable measurement:

| All-ranks component | Sides with a figure / 44 | Paired entries / 22 |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Separate killed | 0 | 0 |
| Separate wounded | 0 | 0 |
| Combined killed and wounded | 16 | 6 |
| Captured | 15 | 1 |
| Separate missing | 0 | 0 |
| Reported aggregate or unsplit personnel loss | 28 | 10 |

Some numerical personnel-loss observation exists on **35/44 sides** and on
both sides of **14/22 entries**. Missingness is asymmetric: **8/22 A sides**
and **1/22 B sides** have no numerical personnel-loss observation. A is France
and its allies, using the unchanged frozen side orientation. These are source
coverage counts, not zero historical casualties or an error-rate estimate.
Combined killed/wounded figures are never split. Detailed officer tables,
omitted from the existing raw transcription, are not all-ranks breakdowns;
their omission is explicit. Headline officer subsets are retained separately.

The six paired killed/wounded entries are `B363a`, `B363b`, `B365c`, `B367a`,
`B368c` and `B369c`. Only **Verona (`B365c`)** has numerical prisoner figures
and both printed killed/wounded-plus-prisoner aggregates with matching
source-declared composition. The ten co-reported total pairs otherwise mix
unspecified composition, blood losses, prisoner subsets and surrender/status
claims. Complete coverage with one shared definition is absent; no arbitrary
sample-size threshold substitutes for that requirement.

The images support three narrative captivity observations: Memmingen's 6,000,
Ulm's 24,000 and Trochtelfingen's 1,700 refer to explicitly named populations
entering captivity. They are source-local co-reference observations, not fills
of the old frame's loss columns. Ulm separately permits conditional officer
return and reports French blockade/bombardment losses over 12–17 October.
Dornbirn's named 4,000 may withdraw freely to Bohemia under a one-year
non-service condition; that strength is not coded as a prisoner loss or proof
of later compliance. Castelfranco retains its fighting-plus-capitulation scope.

Nested quantities stay separate. Hollabrunn's 1,800 prisoners are within its
approximately 3,000 personnel-loss total; no 4,800 total or inferred 1,200
killed/wounded count is created. The Austerlitz 6,000 Austrian losses are within
the 36,000 coalition total, with their own 16,000 Austrian strength denominator.
Officer subsets, guns, flags/eagles, ammunition wagons and horses are not
added into all-ranks losses. **Ten printed personnel sums reconcile**, but
source arithmetic does not prove historically unique people.

All **19 printed personnel-percentage observations** are retained beside
their count arithmetic. The original images confirm, among others:

- Haslach: 1,500 losses / 6,000 strength gives **25%**, while the source prints **32%**.
- Dürnstein: 6,600 / 8,000 gives **82.5%**, while the source prints **88%**.
- Caldiero: 8,000 / 46,000 gives **17.391304%**, while the source prints **18.4%**.

Both printed counts and percentages remain unchanged. Small residuals are not
automatically labelled errors; rounding and alternative unstated denominators
remain possible explanations, not established repairs. For Dürnstein's
arithmetic only, this pilot records the explicit 25,000 / 8,000 strength-table
row with its own citation. The older benchmark selected no denominator for
that entry. Its dataset, frame extracts, eligibility and results are preserved;
the new source-local denominator is not admitted as a predictor or silently
installed into any frozen input. No missing casualty count is inferred from
a percentage.

All 22 existing dossiers are linked by SHA-256, with their outcome-claim IDs
and statuses retained. The three existing disputed outcome pointers are
`B363c/side-a-victory-claim`, `B364a/date-of-captures` and `B367a/koch-indecisive`.
Their underlying non-Bodart passages were **not re-inspected in this pilot**;
they remain unreconciled prior records, not new source-verified alternatives
or silently resolved disputes. No dossier or prepared packet evidence changed.

The [companion notebook](research/napoleonic-1805-loss-audit-v1.ipynb) executes

and denominators matter here; a chart would not add information to this small audit.

All **ten focused tests passed**. They cover complete membership and quantities,
refusal of changed frozen records or a reserved-final ID, literal/value and unit
checks, wrong-entry/source-side anchors, unknown versus an explicit zero fixture,
combined-category preservation, nested subsets, the distinction between

The raw source remains `bodart-1908-transcription-v1`, SHA-256
`bf12437b6d19226ce127ce5d81e59d20358a5b1999ffe00f5ac6a8f8c20ef986`.
The new source-local observation record has SHA-256
`6aab931bff48695c71e4d2a9ef4e52d56e5e081a97494afaadd15f2b5cc776b8`.
No source was added or fetched, so no source-additions entry is needed.
Source selection, rounded and qualified totals, uncertain clock/population,
strength-dependent winner coding and one compiler family remain limitations.
This single complete group does not establish feasibility or coverage across

## Naples objective-and-result pilot v1, 2026-10-20

Completed the owner's accepted bounded next step: an objective/result pilot on
all four engagements in frozen `third-coalition 1806` (Campo Tenese, Maida,
Gaëta and Amantea), using existing pinned sources. The complete group/eight sides
was fixed before the completed encoding. Amantea retains December 1806–February
1807; this source group is neither one appointment nor a corpus-wide yield sample.
The [scope](../data/napoleonic/objective-pilot-v1/scope.json) and
[protocol](../data/napoleonic/objective-pilot-v1/protocol.json) preserve the
accepted request, prior commit and exact input bindings.

The [source-linked table and feasibility decision](research/napoleonic-naples-objective-pilot-v1.md)
retain **19 source-qualified propositions** from **39 adopted passages**, checked
against **26 existing pinned images** and four existing author families (Koch,
Damas, Bunbury and Colletta). Every adopted quotation has a section character
range, locator, raw/metadata hashes and image hashes where applicable. Each entry
uses at most two narrative families plus its frozen Bodart frame context; no
source was fetched or registered. Six adopted passages remain OCR-only, with
that limitation visible. The source-additions log needs no new entry.

All **eight prior objective claims and 49 citation indices** remain accounted for:
22 cite an adopted excerpt or containing context; 27 remain prior-reference-only
pointers. Those pointers are not new verified propositions or resolved disputes.
No dossier, raw transcription, source metadata, existing packet or prior pilot
record was changed. Purpose-specific encoding refines how the existing claims
can be used; it does not silently revise their frozen statuses.

The table separates prescribed action, intended effect, broader goal, expected
consequence, retrospective commentary and inferred disposition. For **7/8 sides**,
a source reports intent before the relevant action; **1/8** retains only an inferred
disposition. Gaëta B also has a distinct later council intention; its early refusal,
the general royal/regency command and the late council stay separate. None of the adopted objectives has a supplied, contemporaneously
dated pre-action document (**0/8**). This is an inspected-evidence finding, not a
claim that such documents never existed; retrospective source accounts can still
support descriptive history. A reported event date does not date its recording
or prove what a commander knew. Eight of 19 success criteria remain null.

Material findings and retained limits:

- **Campo Tenese:** Koch's council directive supports an ordered attack, not by
  itself destruction of the entire corps or conquest of Calabria. His next-day
  pursuit aim remains later than the battle's prior purpose. Damas's plan to
  hold while joining the wings is separate from measured delay. His p. 396
  account reports some joining and an advanced guard not joining; no complete
  junction or no-junction replacement is made. Two quotations from the existing
  full OCR selection were checked against the existing image because the old
  figure transcription omits that paragraph. His later inch-by-inch judgment
  remains commentary, not a documented prior mandate.
- **Maida:** the reported advance, intended camp/flank maneuver, expectation of
  retreat to Catanzaro, general insurrection and relief of Gaëta remain distinct.
  The reported retreat is a qualified directional match, with no verified arrival
  time or attributed causal effect. Movement from the camp prevents a battlefield
  victory from proving the exact intended geometry. The later Gaëta agreement
  and occupation do not show the anticipated relief through abandonment. This
  cross-entry reference is not another battle weight or campaign credit.
- **Gaëta:** a purpose to take the place can be linked to reported surrender on
  18 July and entry on 19 July, with distinct source-qualified events. The printed
  translated capitulation's dateline is an outcome document, not a prior objective
  or appointment date. Its embarkation/non-service terms do not establish later
  compliance or permanent destruction. Speed and redeployment remain unestablished
  as mission achievements. The late council's OCR duration `dix4iait jours*` has no
  allowed pinned p. 243 facsimile; the literal is retained and its numeric duration
  stays null. No deadline or automatic failure is invented. Colletta's early
  higher-command refusal and Koch's speculative origin explanation stay separate;
  Hotz/Storz and exclusive whole-siege command are not reconciled.
- **Amantea:** the renewed attack dispositions and reported capitulation support
  a local capture component. Earlier attempts, the 18 December investment and
  later siege clocks stay distinct. Koch's 33 days and Colletta's 40 excluding
  the first assault are not averaged or made an efficiency denominator. Capture
  does not prove regional pacification: a necessary-condition rationale is not
  sufficiency. The defenders' fidelity language and resistance supply no adopted
  prior operational mandate, deadline or relief criterion; that side remains in
  the denominator with an inferred disposition.

**Decision:** retain the descriptive objective/result components and explicit
gaps. These sources do not defend a common numerical outcome grade, value scale
or aggregation across these different tasks and windows. The relation labels
are not ordinal scores or a tally of successes; unknown is not failure. Local
results do not identify campaign contribution or a commander's causal share.

The [companion audit notebook](research/napoleonic-naples-objective-audit-v1.ipynb)
executes four code cells top to bottom using the standard library. It independently
recounts the eight side keys, provenance/criterion coverage and relation categories,
and checks the material partial/unknown cases. Saved stdout outputs match fresh
execution. Notebook-format fields were validated directly: optional `nbformat`,
`nbclient` and `nbconvert` are absent, and no package was installed. A static HTML
preview generated from the saved notebook was inspected in the in-app browser;
summary, data/scope notes, coverage, criterion table, partial-result checks and
takeaways are readable. Exact tables suit this qualitative, explicitly table-scoped
pilot; no meaningful numeric trend or ordered outcome chart is supplied.

Primary verification corrected the initial timing summary: Colletta's existing
context reports early defensive preparation/refusal and a general royal instruction
to the regency. These now remain separate from the late council purpose. No new
source or historical appointment decision was required.

The ten focused tests check complete scope/side denominators; changed frozen bytes;
source range/metadata/image bindings; prevention of fabricated pre-action documents,
scores, normalized durations and criteria; commentary/resistance distinctions; all
prior-citation dispositions; duplicate/cross-final references; the material partial
cases; and notebook format, input hashes, saved outputs and fresh execution. Model
fitters and outcome-loading functions are patched to confirm they are not invoked.

Full `make check` passed: **433 tests in 248.448 seconds**, followed by all
repository source/data-contract and generated-report checks. `make reproduce`
completed successfully. A preservation audit compared all **8,069 prior tracked
files**: only the Makefile, README, roadmap, research log and build receipt

**5,667 source bindings** and **20 output hashes** are unchanged. The receipt

The final observation SHA-256 is
`eed95ce2214a9aaca05c01a3817980da38bcfa6b4a15ae6d3b2e70d7faf8ce72`.

**Stop here.** No source search, broader extraction, common scalar/ordinal grade,

## Napoleonic 1805 context-code admission audit, 2026-10-20

The owner accepted a bounded audit of attacker posture and fortification across
all 22 frozen `third-coalition 1805` engagements, using existing pinned sources,
with exact proposed-use counts and a go/no-go decision before preparing an
experiment. The scope, original claim/source snapshot and protocol were fixed
before semantic assessment at base commit `e8612ffbefa123d18a22d924fcbeeae8bab81f40`.
The [report](research/napoleonic-1805-context-admission-v1.md),
[artifact](../artifacts/napoleonic-1805-context-admission-v1.json) and
[executed notebook](research/napoleonic-1805-context-admission-audit-v1.ipynb)
retain the complete group and all alternatives.

The proposed profile is retrospective prediction immediately before first hostile
contact belonging to the frozen entry, including outposts. Source-relative ordering
can suffice without an exact clock; neither an old null replacement timestamp nor
a later publication date is itself a reason to exclude. Works beyond the first
outpost hit can qualify if they were already held within the reported engagement
positions. A charge may be hostile contact without shots; Parsdorf remains blocked
because the preceding pursuit may belong to the same action, not because its
battalion reportedly surrendered without a blow.

| Unit | Eligible candidate | Blocked | Excluded | Total |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Posture fields | 0 | 0 | 22 | 22 |
| Fortification fields | 6 | 16 | 0 | 22 |
| Original source claims | 6 | 20 | 25 | 51 |

**No-go for preparing the unchanged joint experiment: zero complete two-field
rows.** All 24 realized-posture claims describe action during the engagement,
not unchanged pre-contact intent. This is a variable/use mismatch, not a claim
that prior intentions cannot be researched. The other excluded claim is
Schönhals's later occupation of Schloss St Sigmund at Mariazell; another unknown
alternative keeps the whole fortification field blocked. Alternatives and
repeated citations are not extra rows, votes or scenario weights.

The six fortification candidates are Neresheim (`none_named`), Verona
(`both_fortified`), Caldiero (`B_fortified`), Scharnitz (`B_fortified`), Austerlitz
(`A_fortified`) and Steken (`none_named`). These are source-qualified named-work
categories. `none_named` means no works named in inspected descriptions of both
initial positions; it never supplies physical absence or two zero indicators.
Positive categories likewise do not establish the other side's absence or the
proportion of the force protected. Caldiero's already-held line and Austerlitz's
Santon qualify before initial contact elsewhere. Scharnitz's 4/5 November and
Verona's 17/18 October alternatives are retained. Hohenems construction before
contact is not mislabelled late: Dornbirn remains blocked for position/operation
scope and the alternative main-body location. Sources and coding can remain
historically wrong despite exact quotations.

The primary agent inspected **405 prior citation occurrences / 374 distinct
citation objects**, plus **five existing-source context excerpts**. All **91 text
sources and 77 associated image records** retain exact raw and complete metadata
hashes. **Ten images were visually re-inspected** here; other facsimiles are
byte-bound earlier records, not new visual checks. The 379 passage records retain
